# Risk management and sizing

1909 claims from 215 sources. Each line links to the claim inside its source note.


## [[MT001 - 133 - Jan van Eck- Macro Forces Shape Markets]]
- [[MT001 - 133 - Jan van Eck- Macro Forces Shape Markets#^MT001-C05-5|MT001-C05-5]] — Stay fully invested in equities through cycles because timing is hard, except when the Fed is raising rates sharply.
- [[MT001 - 133 - Jan van Eck- Macro Forces Shape Markets#^MT001-C05-6|MT001-C05-6]] — The 60/40 premise rests on bond yield and inverse stock-bond correlation; he is biased against it and prefers real assets as a diversifier given current risks.
- [[MT001 - 133 - Jan van Eck- Macro Forces Shape Markets#^MT001-C05-6b|MT001-C05-6b]] — 60/40 rests on two premises: bonds earn a competitive return through their yield, and stocks and bonds are inversely correlated so bonds balance the portfolio.
- [[MT001 - 133 - Jan van Eck- Macro Forces Shape Markets#^MT001-C05-7|MT001-C05-7]] — Illustrative allocation: 60-70% equities, about 10% real assets, remainder bonds; he says there is very little science behind it.
- [[MT001 - 133 - Jan van Eck- Macro Forces Shape Markets#^MT001-C06-1|MT001-C06-1]] — Rebalancing on a calendar (quarterly) or price-trigger basis is a practical tool for resilience in unpredictable markets.
- [[MT001 - 133 - Jan van Eck- Macro Forces Shape Markets#^MT001-C06-2|MT001-C06-2]] — In a commodities portfolio, harvesting gold gains last year and reinvesting in oil at $55-60 paid off this year, an example of rebalancing into the laggard.
- [[MT001 - 133 - Jan van Eck- Macro Forces Shape Markets#^MT001-C06-4b|MT001-C06-4b]] — Resilience is hard to optimize directly, so hold assets you will be fine owning in the end and accept interim price volatility.
- [[MT001 - 133 - Jan van Eck- Macro Forces Shape Markets#^MT001-C06-5|MT001-C06-5]] — Resilience comes from broad diversification across assets that win in different environments, giving up some upside to protect the downside, since the alternative is accurate timing.
- [[MT001 - 133 - Jan van Eck- Macro Forces Shape Markets#^MT001-C06-6|MT001-C06-6]] — Private fund software holdings are marked down when public SaaS stocks fall, so private assets add less diversification than their smooth marks suggest.
- [[MT001 - 133 - Jan van Eck- Macro Forces Shape Markets#^MT001-C06-7|MT001-C06-7]] — Judge diversification by underlying risk exposures, not by number of holdings or labels.
- [[MT001 - 133 - Jan van Eck- Macro Forces Shape Markets#^MT001-C08-7|MT001-C08-7]] — Size narrative positions small, dip a toe and keep an eye on valuation.
- [[MT001 - 133 - Jan van Eck- Macro Forces Shape Markets#^MT001-C09-2|MT001-C09-2]] — Avoid products whose behaviour differs from the name (leveraged, inverse, dislocated or expensive ETFs) so clients are not surprised.

## [[MT002 - 2024 Trend Following Recap- Key Insights from Top Traders - Systematic Investor 327]]
- [[MT002 - 2024 Trend Following Recap- Key Insights from Top Traders - Systematic Investor 327#^MT002-C03-2|MT002-C03-2]] — Trend replication using a recent return window de-risks more slowly than managers with stop-losses and vol controls, so it lagged on the August 2024 drop but recovered by month-end because it did not cut exposure at the bottom.
- [[MT002 - 2024 Trend Following Recap- Key Insights from Top Traders - Systematic Investor 327#^MT002-C08-3|MT002-C08-3]] — The benefit of adding alternative markets is diversification, not stronger trends.
- [[MT002 - 2024 Trend Following Recap- Key Insights from Top Traders - Systematic Investor 327#^MT002-C09-3|MT002-C09-3]] — Managers trading hundreds of markets gain little from outlier moves in a few small markets because each market has too little weight; those who did well traded few markets.
- [[MT002 - 2024 Trend Following Recap- Key Insights from Top Traders - Systematic Investor 327#^MT002-C15-1|MT002-C15-1]] — Some systematic managers discretionarily de-risked over the weekend of the August 2024 shock, adding to dispersion.

## [[MT003 - 40 Years of Trading Lessons with Patrick Welton - Top Traders Unplugged - Ep.153]]
- [[MT003 - 40 Years of Trading Lessons with Patrick Welton - Top Traders Unplugged - Ep.153#^MT003-C02-1|MT003-C02-1]] — Commodities Corporation, an early multistrategy pod shop, scaled each trader's capital in steps as return targets were hit, a performance-gated allocation scheme.
- [[MT003 - 40 Years of Trading Lessons with Patrick Welton - Top Traders Unplugged - Ep.153#^MT003-C04-1|MT003-C04-1]] — Once a manager takes outside capital, the client's goals (not the manager's own) must drive risk and program design, which changes how risk is managed.
- [[MT003 - 40 Years of Trading Lessons with Patrick Welton - Top Traders Unplugged - Ep.153#^MT003-C04-2|MT003-C04-2]] — Allocators who combine managers want constant, volatility-targeted exposure, whereas investors want exposure scaled up when forward return is better and down when it is weaker.
- [[MT003 - 40 Years of Trading Lessons with Patrick Welton - Top Traders Unplugged - Ep.153#^MT003-C04-4|MT003-C04-4]] — Welton's early trend program let volatility float, averaging about 15% in the 1990s, about 10% when opportunity was thin and about 20% when it was rich, rather than targeting a fixed level.
- [[MT003 - 40 Years of Trading Lessons with Patrick Welton - Top Traders Unplugged - Ep.153#^MT003-C05-2|MT003-C05-2]] — Rising volatility often comes with rising correlation, so without risk scaling net portfolio exposure rises just before reversals, producing fast losses.
- [[MT003 - 40 Years of Trading Lessons with Patrick Welton - Top Traders Unplugged - Ep.153#^MT003-C05-3|MT003-C05-3]] — In the early 2000s managers began volatility targeting the whole portfolio or segments of it to mute the fast-loss pattern and suit professional allocators.
- [[MT003 - 40 Years of Trading Lessons with Patrick Welton - Top Traders Unplugged - Ep.153#^MT003-C06-5|MT003-C06-5]] — Even though pure trend following has a return shape few investors like, its properties are very useful alongside a traditional stock-bond portfolio.
- [[MT003 - 40 Years of Trading Lessons with Patrick Welton - Top Traders Unplugged - Ep.153#^MT003-C07-2|MT003-C07-2]] — Position sizing should include forward-looking opportunity as well as risk, not just current volatility.
- [[MT003 - 40 Years of Trading Lessons with Patrick Welton - Top Traders Unplugged - Ep.153#^MT003-C07-3|MT003-C07-3]] — The symmetry question asks whether a trade has an asymmetric win/loss profile, either directly or synthetically, rather than an equal win to loss balance.
- [[MT003 - 40 Years of Trading Lessons with Patrick Welton - Top Traders Unplugged - Ep.153#^MT003-C11-3|MT003-C11-3]] — Relative value baskets neutralized for dollar risk, rate duration (DV01) or broad commodity exposure add diversification without stacking on the main portfolio's thematic risks.
- [[MT003 - 40 Years of Trading Lessons with Patrick Welton - Top Traders Unplugged - Ep.153#^MT003-C12-3|MT003-C12-3]] — Allocators who care about adverse multi-quarter moves, such as pensions facing a two-sigma liability shock, need better tail coverage, and trend following gives it if sized properly (portable alpha, volatility level).
- [[MT003 - 40 Years of Trading Lessons with Patrick Welton - Top Traders Unplugged - Ep.153#^MT003-C13-4|MT003-C13-4]] — A manager can detect a capacity problem when objectively measured execution costs trend upward, usually first in a few markets before all.

## [[MT004 - 5% Rates, Bessent's Failed Gamble & What Comes Next w- Krishna Guha - The Real Eisman Play]]
- [[MT004 - 5% Rates, Bessent's Failed Gamble & What Comes Next w- Krishna Guha - The Real Eisman Play#^MT004-C06-1|MT004-C06-1]] — Strong forward guidance (firm commitments to hike a set number of times) is risky because the world is uncertain and the bank can get locked into wrong actions, so it should be an emergency tool only.

## [[MT005 - A Brief World Economic History For Gold, Silver, and Fiat Money]]
- [[MT005 - A Brief World Economic History For Gold, Silver, and Fiat Money#^MT005-C01-5|MT005-C01-5]] — Of about 3600 listed mining and metal stocks most hold only claims and a hope of a deposit, so the opportunity set is large but selection quality (expert geological vetting) determines results.
- [[MT005 - A Brief World Economic History For Gold, Silver, and Fiat Money#^MT005-C03-5|MT005-C03-5]] — Many Comstock-era mining stocks were scams built on insider trading and bogus discovery stories, so early mining equity investing carries fraud risk that needs independent verification.
- [[MT005 - A Brief World Economic History For Gold, Silver, and Fiat Money#^MT005-C03-6|MT005-C03-6]] — Twain lost most of his fortune on speculative new-technology ventures such as the Paige compositor, which a competing technology made obsolete, a cautionary tale about betting on unproven technology.
- [[MT005 - A Brief World Economic History For Gold, Silver, and Fiat Money#^MT005-C05-3|MT005-C05-3]] — The Bre-X fraud, a claimed 200 million ounce Busang gold deposit with samples that were salted, was one of the biggest corporate frauds in Canadian history, so exploration stock results rest on trustworthy sampling and drilling data.

## [[MT006 - A Challenging October for Trend Following - Systematic Investor 317]]
- [[MT006 - A Challenging October for Trend Following - Systematic Investor 317#^MT006-C04-2|MT006-C04-2]] — Holding SPY, TLT and EEM (equities, long bonds, emerging markets) diversifies across weakly correlated asset classes and smooths returns, similar to all-weather portfolios.
- [[MT006 - A Challenging October for Trend Following - Systematic Investor 317#^MT006-C05-2|MT006-C05-2]] — A long-only moving-average overlay limits large drawdowns by exiting assets in prolonged downtrends.
- [[MT006 - A Challenging October for Trend Following - Systematic Investor 317#^MT006-C05-4|MT006-C05-4]] — A trend-following overlay is slow risk mitigation, not an immediate hedge; options are the tool for short-term tail protection.
- [[MT006 - A Challenging October for Trend Following - Systematic Investor 317#^MT006-C05-5|MT006-C05-5]] — Applying the trend filter separately to several uncorrelated asset classes lets the portfolio rotate dynamically toward whichever asset is trending.
- [[MT006 - A Challenging October for Trend Following - Systematic Investor 317#^MT006-C06-X1|MT006-C06-X1]] — Despite its caveats (whipsaw, long-only, regime bias) both hosts judge the Faber-style long-only trend overlay a valid strategy, likely better than unmanaged buy-and-hold.
- [[MT006 - A Challenging October for Trend Following - Systematic Investor 317#^MT006-C07-3|MT006-C07-3]] — Start with small bets and concentrate capital progressively as a trend persists, so capital stays mostly inactive during noise.
- [[MT006 - A Challenging October for Trend Following - Systematic Investor 317#^MT006-C07-4|MT006-C07-4]] — Staggered activation of multiple uncorrelated entry systems (Donchian, Keltner, Bollinger breakouts) builds position size only as a trend endures.
- [[MT006 - A Challenging October for Trend Following - Systematic Investor 317#^MT006-C08-3|MT006-C08-3]] — Diversify broadly to find the markets in outlier trends, then concentrate risk there.
- [[MT006 - A Challenging October for Trend Following - Systematic Investor 317#^MT006-C09-1|MT006-C09-1]] — The number of ensemble systems is limited in practice by futures margin when risk concentrates in expensive-margin markets (e.g. palladium).
- [[MT006 - A Challenging October for Trend Following - Systematic Investor 317#^MT006-C09-3|MT006-C09-3]] — Every system in the ensemble uses a trailing stop so open profits are not given back.
- [[MT006 - A Challenging October for Trend Following - Systematic Investor 317#^MT006-C09-X1|MT006-C09-X1]] — Highly successful discretionary equity investors run very concentrated books (Buffett about 40% in Apple, Ackman 53% of a $10bn fund in three stocks, Nomad Investment Partners in one or two stocks), raising the question of how much concentration a trend system should allow.
- [[MT006 - A Challenging October for Trend Following - Systematic Investor 317#^MT006-C10-1|MT006-C10-1]] — An ensemble of many systems with individually tighter stops gives an aggregate looser trailing exit than two systems with wide stops.
- [[MT006 - A Challenging October for Trend Following - Systematic Investor 317#^MT006-C10-2|MT006-C10-2]] — Adding more uncorrelated systems continuously improves drawdown and gives a small, diminishing MAR/CAGR increase with no sharp stopping point.
- [[MT006 - A Challenging October for Trend Following - Systematic Investor 317#^MT006-C12-2|MT006-C12-2]] — Boundaries such as risk rules and regulation limit the degrees of freedom of agents; a trend strategy has position limits, stops and leverage caps.

## [[MT007 - A Financial Regime Shift Is Coming - Systematic Investor 332]]
- [[MT007 - A Financial Regime Shift Is Coming - Systematic Investor 332#^MT007-C03-1|MT007-C03-1]] — Risk has a measurable part (volatility) and a non-measurable part (uncertainty); volatility alone understates risk when uncertainty is high.
- [[MT007 - A Financial Regime Shift Is Coming - Systematic Investor 332#^MT007-C03-2|MT007-C03-2]] — Early 2025: stable volatility but very high uncertainty means true risk is higher than volatility indicates.
- [[MT007 - A Financial Regime Shift Is Coming - Systematic Investor 332#^MT007-C03-5|MT007-C03-5]] — Missing a regime change causes the biggest losses because of excess leverage, under-diversification, wrong correlations and missed trends, so measure regime change to rebalance risk.
- [[MT007 - A Financial Regime Shift Is Coming - Systematic Investor 332#^MT007-C04-1|MT007-C04-1]] — A regime change with higher equity and bond volatility raises the volatility of a 60/40 allocation.
- [[MT007 - A Financial Regime Shift Is Coming - Systematic Investor 332#^MT007-C05-2|MT007-C05-2]] — Managers recalibrate correlation matrices on a fixed calendar (e.g. six months) while position sizing uses short-term volatility, so regime changes enter risk estimates with a lag; shorten the update period.
- [[MT007 - A Financial Regime Shift Is Coming - Systematic Investor 332#^MT007-C11-4|MT007-C11-4]] — Beta flex: hold flexible equity beta, higher when the market rises and negative when it falls, which means convexity.
- [[MT007 - A Financial Regime Shift Is Coming - Systematic Investor 332#^MT007-C14-3|MT007-C14-3]] — Prime broker risk models measure volatility well but depend on assumed correlations; the non-measurable uncertainty is what breaks leveraged books (carry unwind, Mag 7 losses).
- [[MT007 - A Financial Regime Shift Is Coming - Systematic Investor 332#^MT007-C16-1|MT007-C16-1]] — Due diligence question: how does the model respond when correlations go to one.

## [[MT008 - AI Bubble Peak- - Systematic Investor - Ep.388]]
- [[MT008 - AI Bubble Peak- - Systematic Investor - Ep.388#^MT008-C04-1|MT008-C04-1]] — Risk is countable (volatility, VIX) while uncertainty is not, because there are no past events to count.
- [[MT008 - AI Bubble Peak- - Systematic Investor - Ep.388#^MT008-C08-5|MT008-C08-5]] — When stock-bond correlation turns positive and rates are lower, hold more alternatives; when strongly negative with high rates, alternatives are less needed.
- [[MT008 - AI Bubble Peak- - Systematic Investor - Ep.388#^MT008-C08-6|MT008-C08-6]] — Regime knowledge adjusts trend-based portfolio tilts to avoid overexposure to one sector.

## [[MT009 - AI Can Build Your Portfolio—But Can You Trust It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.419]]
- [[MT009 - AI Can Build Your Portfolio—But Can You Trust It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.419#^MT009-C03-5|MT009-C03-5]] — Adding trend following to a 60/40 portfolio offsets the long bond core with a short bond position in a rate sell-off.
- [[MT009 - AI Can Build Your Portfolio—But Can You Trust It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.419#^MT009-C06-2|MT009-C06-2]] — The design uses equal notional with no volatility scaling, so risk differs hugely between S&P and Treasuries.
- [[MT009 - AI Can Build Your Portfolio—But Can You Trust It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.419#^MT009-C07-4|MT009-C07-4]] — Scale trend signals continuously by volatility (more volatile means weaker signal), instead of switching windows with triggers.
- [[MT009 - AI Can Build Your Portfolio—But Can You Trust It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.419#^MT009-C07-5|MT009-C07-5]] — Volatility and correlation spikes together change the capital required for a vol target, so portfolio design should use both rather than the VIX.
- [[MT009 - AI Can Build Your Portfolio—But Can You Trust It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.419#^MT009-C08-1|MT009-C08-1]] — Do not over-parameterise trend following in trendless periods; add complementary strategies that benefit from those regimes.
- [[MT009 - AI Can Build Your Portfolio—But Can You Trust It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.419#^MT009-C11-1|MT009-C11-1]] — The method set spans heuristics (equal weight, cap weight, inverse vol), return-based (mean-variance, Black-Litterman), risk-based (risk parity, max diversification, minimum variance) and non-traditional (CVaR, drawdown constraints).
- [[MT009 - AI Can Build Your Portfolio—But Can You Trust It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.419#^MT009-C11-2|MT009-C11-2]] — An adversarial diversifier agent builds the principal component of the other portfolios and takes the opposite stance to find unseen correlation pockets.
- [[MT009 - AI Can Build Your Portfolio—But Can You Trust It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.419#^MT009-C13-2|MT009-C13-2]] — When the macro regime is unclear, diversify more and rely less on return-based capital market assumptions.
- [[MT009 - AI Can Build Your Portfolio—But Can You Trust It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.419#^MT009-C13-3|MT009-C13-3]] — The CIO favoured cap-weight, vol-target, equal-weight and inverse-variance portfolios that use no return forecasts.
- [[MT009 - AI Can Build Your Portfolio—But Can You Trust It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.419#^MT009-C15-3|MT009-C15-3]] — Trend portfolio construction uses one or two methods, so the multi-method agent approach could add value there.

## [[MT010 - AI Could Change Markets Forever - Systematic Investor - Ep.417]]
- [[MT010 - AI Could Change Markets Forever - Systematic Investor - Ep.417#^MT010-C03-2|MT010-C03-2]] — ETF wrappers carry a risk that the strategy or the subadvisor changes without notice, unlike a fund run by the actual advisor.
- [[MT010 - AI Could Change Markets Forever - Systematic Investor - Ep.417#^MT010-C04-1|MT010-C04-1]] — According to Seeking Alpha the ETF went from over 50 markets to energy only, losing diversification across many markets, which is why low-Sharpe strategies like trend following make money.
- [[MT010 - AI Could Change Markets Forever - Systematic Investor - Ep.417#^MT010-C10-3|MT010-C10-3]] — Non-leveraged traders must rank positions and use path-dependent swap rules.
- [[MT010 - AI Could Change Markets Forever - Systematic Investor - Ep.417#^MT010-C12-1|MT010-C12-1]] — Trend models enter at about the same time; managers differ in risk management and exit.
- [[MT010 - AI Could Change Markets Forever - Systematic Investor - Ep.417#^MT010-C12-2|MT010-C12-2]] — Where discretion is retained, use a simple stop-loss to hand exits to a rule.
- [[MT010 - AI Could Change Markets Forever - Systematic Investor - Ep.417#^MT010-C12-3|MT010-C12-3]] — Biggest debate: whether to resize positions for volatility after entry or let them ride.
- [[MT010 - AI Could Change Markets Forever - Systematic Investor - Ep.417#^MT010-C13-2|MT010-C13-2]] — AI code review found a serious bug but introduced a worse one that would have deleted all short positions, so AI-written code needs oversight.
- [[MT010 - AI Could Change Markets Forever - Systematic Investor - Ep.417#^MT010-C13-3|MT010-C13-3]] — Governance and code oversight apply equally to AI-assisted systems.
- [[MT010 - AI Could Change Markets Forever - Systematic Investor - Ep.417#^MT010-C15-2|MT010-C15-2]] — Rob trades less momentum when volatility is high, while the article trades faster when VIX is high.
- [[MT010 - AI Could Change Markets Forever - Systematic Investor - Ep.417#^MT010-C18-1|MT010-C18-1]] — Diversify across managers since picking the best is hard; quant equity offers rising idiosyncratic performance.

## [[MT011 - AI, Inflation, and the Portfolio That Refuses to Sit Still - Systematic Investor - Ep.399]]
- [[MT011 - AI, Inflation, and the Portfolio That Refuses to Sit Still - Systematic Investor - Ep.399#^MT011-C04-5|MT011-C04-5]] — Trend followers were offset in the rate shock by strong energy trends, so cross-asset breadth cushions losses in one sector.
- [[MT011 - AI, Inflation, and the Portfolio That Refuses to Sit Still - Systematic Investor - Ep.399#^MT011-C04-X1|MT011-C04-X1]] — In a strong energy-trend period, trend managers running very high volatility targets produced far stronger returns than peers, so the volatility target scales the payoff of trend episodes.
- [[MT011 - AI, Inflation, and the Portfolio That Refuses to Sit Still - Systematic Investor - Ep.399#^MT011-C06-5|MT011-C06-5]] — Simultaneous positive supply (AI) and negative supply (energy) shocks have no historical precedent, making policy and model-based inference especially uncertain.
- [[MT011 - AI, Inflation, and the Portfolio That Refuses to Sit Still - Systematic Investor - Ep.399#^MT011-C10-5|MT011-C10-5]] — Allowing long-short and leverage (return stacking) adds flexibility versus long-only multi-asset portfolios.
- [[MT011 - AI, Inflation, and the Portfolio That Refuses to Sit Still - Systematic Investor - Ep.399#^MT011-C11-7|MT011-C11-7]] — A stock-bond portfolio is short inflation risk, so trend and commodities are needed as an inflation hedge.
- [[MT011 - AI, Inflation, and the Portfolio That Refuses to Sit Still - Systematic Investor - Ep.399#^MT011-C12-1|MT011-C12-1]] — Even well-diversified portfolios occasionally lose in all components at once by chance, as in March 2026 and 'death by a thousand cuts'; this is not a construction failure.
- [[MT011 - AI, Inflation, and the Portfolio That Refuses to Sit Still - Systematic Investor - Ep.399#^MT011-C12-3|MT011-C12-3]] — Diversifying to avoid large equity bear markets or a lost decade means accepting periodic simultaneous losses.
- [[MT011 - AI, Inflation, and the Portfolio That Refuses to Sit Still - Systematic Investor - Ep.399#^MT011-C12-X1|MT011-C12-X1]] — Correlations converging to one in stress is more likely for some strategies than others, so diversifiers differ in how reliably they hold up in crises.
- [[MT011 - AI, Inflation, and the Portfolio That Refuses to Sit Still - Systematic Investor - Ep.399#^MT011-C13-1|MT011-C13-1]] — Market wizards' track records include 30 to 50 percent drawdowns, so large returns require tolerance for discomfort.
- [[MT011 - AI, Inflation, and the Portfolio That Refuses to Sit Still - Systematic Investor - Ep.399#^MT011-C13-4|MT011-C13-4]] — Return stacking (trend on top of equities or bonds) was popularised after 2020 to bring trend to wealth investors.
- [[MT011 - AI, Inflation, and the Portfolio That Refuses to Sit Still - Systematic Investor - Ep.399#^MT011-C14-4|MT011-C14-4]] — Excluding equities from trend would change the correlation matrix and risk budget, reducing capacity for bond exposure that can add returns.
- [[MT011 - AI, Inflation, and the Portfolio That Refuses to Sit Still - Systematic Investor - Ep.399#^MT011-C14-5|MT011-C14-5]] — SAA + trend + currency and commodity trend yields a regime-adaptive portfolio.
- [[MT011 - AI, Inflation, and the Portfolio That Refuses to Sit Still - Systematic Investor - Ep.399#^MT011-C16-1|MT011-C16-1]] — The Regime Adaptive Fund allocates risk 40% growth, 20% diversifiers (bonds/gold) and 40% trend following, sized by volatility and medium-term correlations.
- [[MT011 - AI, Inflation, and the Portfolio That Refuses to Sit Still - Systematic Investor - Ep.399#^MT011-C16-5|MT011-C16-5]] — The real risk is a 50 percent equity drawdown or lost decade (2008, 1970s), not short episodes like 2022 or COVID, so a pre-defined response matters.
- [[MT011 - AI, Inflation, and the Portfolio That Refuses to Sit Still - Systematic Investor - Ep.399#^MT011-C17-2|MT011-C17-2]] — Expected volatility of the Regime Adaptive Fund is 10-12%, about two-thirds of equity volatility.
- [[MT011 - AI, Inflation, and the Portfolio That Refuses to Sit Still - Systematic Investor - Ep.399#^MT011-C17-3|MT011-C17-3]] — Equities keep making new highs despite geopolitical risk; investors need participation with protection, which a trend/tactical sleeve provides.

## [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387]]
- [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387#^MT013-C03-2|MT013-C03-2]] — Dollar weakness cut a euro investor's 2025 S&P 500 return to about 2.5% versus 16% in USD, so currency exposure dominates foreign returns.
- [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387#^MT013-C08-3|MT013-C08-3]] — With few trending markets in 2025, risk allocation and risk management decided manager returns.
- [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387#^MT013-C08-4|MT013-C08-4]] — Manager returns in early 2026 ranged about 8-15% YTD, showing good risk management.
- [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387#^MT013-C08-5|MT013-C08-5]] — Unmanaged trend portfolios swing heavily in precious metals, while actual CTAs cut risk quickly during the late-January 2026 selloff.
- [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387#^MT013-C08-6|MT013-C08-6]] — Silver risk allocation in CTAs was small relative to gold because it is a smaller market.
- [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387#^MT013-C08-7|MT013-C08-7]] — CTAs use proprietary trend-strength and overextension models to size down late in trends.
- [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387#^MT013-C09-3|MT013-C09-3]] — Short-term trend models have no strong Sharpe and are costly risk management tools that raise trading costs.
- [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387#^MT013-C09-4|MT013-C09-4]] — Allocating risk to a zero-Sharpe component to reduce volatility is a poor trade.
- [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387#^MT013-C09-X1|MT013-C09-X1]] — Despite the headlines, CTAs had less risk in precious metals than assumed; their VaR allocation was much larger in equities and FX.
- [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387#^MT013-C10-1|MT013-C10-1]] — A modern trend follower with variable position sizing sold precious metals as volatility rose, giving up some profit.
- [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387#^MT013-C10-2|MT013-C10-2]] — Stops triggered on Friday execute Monday, so weekend-style gaps can destroy managers who do not cut risk with volatility.
- [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387#^MT013-C10-3|MT013-C10-3]] — Managers with static positions and hundreds of markets get little from outliers; to make 50-80% returns you need few markets and a large risk allocation.
- [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387#^MT013-C10-4|MT013-C10-4]] — Concentrated trend programs create return but are hard to sell given investment committee constraints and strategic allocations.
- [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387#^MT013-C11-1|MT013-C11-1]] — Investors whose core risk is equities want asymmetric diversification, which trend delivers better than multi-strategy funds.
- [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387#^MT013-C12-2|MT013-C12-2]] — Risk management should not be conflated with allocating heavily to a zero-Sharpe strategy.
- [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387#^MT013-C12-4|MT013-C12-4]] — Adding many non-core markets increases drawdowns during de-risking because of implementation costs.
- [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387#^MT013-C12-X2|MT013-C12-X2]] — Slow (weekly) de-risking helped around Liberation Day because the sell-off reversed quickly; faster de-risking sold near the low.
- [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387#^MT013-C13-3|MT013-C13-3]] — Hedging a long-term trend model with out-of-the-money puts out of fear of tail events is a losing business.
- [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387#^MT013-C18-1|MT013-C18-1]] — Macro funds use bank QIS indices for thematic bets like curve steepeners, outsourcing execution and financing.
- [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387#^MT013-C20-3|MT013-C20-3]] — Sophisticated allocators now combine several banks' flavours of trend to diversify design risk.

## [[MT014 - Adapt or Perish- Why Systematic Investors Must Evolve in 2024 - Systematic Investor 325]]
- [[MT014 - Adapt or Perish- Why Systematic Investors Must Evolve in 2024 - Systematic Investor 325#^MT014-C04-4|MT014-C04-4]] — Capacity analysis should quantify the Sharpe degradation as assets grow.
- [[MT014 - Adapt or Perish- Why Systematic Investors Must Evolve in 2024 - Systematic Investor 325#^MT014-C05-1|MT014-C05-1]] — Trading harder-to-access OTC alternative markets requires execution investment and higher costs.
- [[MT014 - Adapt or Perish- Why Systematic Investors Must Evolve in 2024 - Systematic Investor 325#^MT014-C07-4|MT014-C07-4]] — In a one-week shock the best action was nothing; protection against such events requires a paying long-vol strategy.
- [[MT014 - Adapt or Perish- Why Systematic Investors Must Evolve in 2024 - Systematic Investor 325#^MT014-C08-1|MT014-C08-1]] — For very short crises trend followers' volatility filters do not change positions in the first days, only a few days later.
- [[MT014 - Adapt or Perish- Why Systematic Investors Must Evolve in 2024 - Systematic Investor 325#^MT014-C08-3|MT014-C08-3]] — A three-layer defence: long vol first (costly), quality long-short second for short bursts, trend third.
- [[MT014 - Adapt or Perish- Why Systematic Investors Must Evolve in 2024 - Systematic Investor 325#^MT014-C08-4|MT014-C08-4]] — A 50/50 stocks and trend allocation gives almost the sum of the two returns, though both can be long equities into a crisis from all-time highs.
- [[MT014 - Adapt or Perish- Why Systematic Investors Must Evolve in 2024 - Systematic Investor 325#^MT014-C08-X2|MT014-C08-X2]] — Like Druckenmiller's concentrated bets, trend followers make their big gains when the portfolio is concentrated in a few large positions; higher risk and volatility come with the reward.
- [[MT014 - Adapt or Perish- Why Systematic Investors Must Evolve in 2024 - Systematic Investor 325#^MT014-C09-2|MT014-C09-2]] — Protecting against short shocks costs long-term return; responsive vol scaling cuts positions on a Yenmageddon event and misses the rebound.
- [[MT014 - Adapt or Perish- Why Systematic Investors Must Evolve in 2024 - Systematic Investor 325#^MT014-C11-5|MT014-C11-5]] — A more unpredictable largest economy argues for a strategy that makes no predictions and follows markets.
- [[MT014 - Adapt or Perish- Why Systematic Investors Must Evolve in 2024 - Systematic Investor 325#^MT014-C12-2|MT014-C12-2]] — By conventional metrics (Sharpe, drawdown) investors should hold more trend; the exact amount varies but 'probably more than you've got'.
- [[MT014 - Adapt or Perish- Why Systematic Investors Must Evolve in 2024 - Systematic Investor 325#^MT014-C13-1|MT014-C13-1]] — Balter's insight: trend breaks the asset-allocation mold and should be sized by tracking error versus benchmark.
- [[MT014 - Adapt or Perish- Why Systematic Investors Must Evolve in 2024 - Systematic Investor 325#^MT014-C13-3|MT014-C13-3]] — In April 2024 equity weakness hurt trend equity positions but bonds, currencies and commodities offset it.
- [[MT014 - Adapt or Perish- Why Systematic Investors Must Evolve in 2024 - Systematic Investor 325#^MT014-C14-3|MT014-C14-3]] — Even a modest Sharpe of 0.2 for trend implies about a 10% allocation.

## [[MT015 - Ajay Banga - President of World Bank - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment]]
- [[MT015 - Ajay Banga - President of World Bank - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment#^MT015-C06-2|MT015-C06-2]] — A concentrated stock-paid holding in a single bank collapsed in 2008, showing concentration risk; absence of debt provided stability.

## [[MT016 - America's 250-Year Reset feat. Cem Karsan - Systematic Investor - Ep. 407]]
- [[MT016 - America's 250-Year Reset feat. Cem Karsan - Systematic Investor - Ep. 407#^MT016-C03-5|MT016-C03-5]] — Long-vol hedges earned nothing in the Q1 2026 drawdown (about 9-10%) because the decline was slow and grinding, and the fast rebound then hurt them.
- [[MT016 - America's 250-Year Reset feat. Cem Karsan - Systematic Investor - Ep. 407#^MT016-C03-6|MT016-C03-6]] — Systematic long-vol hedging is chronically frustrating because it pays only in sudden moves, and hedges have repeatedly failed (2022, April 2025, 2026).
- [[MT016 - America's 250-Year Reset feat. Cem Karsan - Systematic Investor - Ep. 407#^MT016-C04-2|MT016-C04-2]] — Volatility strategies are the distribution of all outcomes and the convex bet that can win from either tail; right-tail players did well in 2026.

## [[MT017 - Are Markets Becoming Living Systems- - Systematic Investor - Ep. 402]]
- [[MT017 - Are Markets Becoming Living Systems- - Systematic Investor - Ep. 402#^MT017-C09-1|MT017-C09-1]] — Trend following already controls instrument variance: ATR sizing shrinks a position automatically when variance rises, plus portfolio vol targeting and position-level drawdown management.
- [[MT017 - Are Markets Becoming Living Systems- - Systematic Investor - Ep. 402#^MT017-C09-2|MT017-C09-2]] — Correlation shows how shocks travel across instruments (diagnostic); variance within instruments is the control for sizing, cutting and backing off; a trend follower needs both.

## [[MT018 - BG2 Thanksgiving Roundtable- DOGE, EV Tax Credit, Tariffs, Conspiracy Theories, Invest Ame]]
- [[MT018 - BG2 Thanksgiving Roundtable- DOGE, EV Tax Credit, Tariffs, Conspiracy Theories, Invest Ame#^MT018-C04-2|MT018-C04-2]] — Companies accept such terms to avoid a down round, but it concentrates risk and can lead to negative outcomes later.
- [[MT018 - BG2 Thanksgiving Roundtable- DOGE, EV Tax Credit, Tariffs, Conspiracy Theories, Invest Ame#^MT018-C09-1|MT018-C09-1]] — For root-cause analysis under uncertainty (COVID origins) treat both competing hypotheses as real, and avoid labelling dissent a conspiracy theory.

## [[MT019 - Backtesting Can't Predict The Future - Systematic Investor 310]]
- [[MT019 - Backtesting Can't Predict The Future - Systematic Investor 310#^MT019-C02-1|MT019-C02-1]] — A quick volatile event makes vol-scaled trend followers more defensive in positioning, so the next shock hurts less; expect performance dispersion and model speed to matter.
- [[MT019 - Backtesting Can't Predict The Future - Systematic Investor 310#^MT019-C02-3|MT019-C02-3]] — Volatility-targeted trend followers reduce exposure after a vol rise even if prices recover.
- [[MT019 - Backtesting Can't Predict The Future - Systematic Investor 310#^MT019-C02-4|MT019-C02-4]] — Analogy: severe turbulence hurts those inside but not the structure; similarly trend-following drawdowns are painful but do not break the models.
- [[MT019 - Backtesting Can't Predict The Future - Systematic Investor 310#^MT019-C02-5|MT019-C02-5]] — No market event breaks a rule-based trend system with sensible, tested rules (not AI-optimised); convergence strategies and systematic macro are the ones that tend to break.
- [[MT019 - Backtesting Can't Predict The Future - Systematic Investor 310#^MT019-C02-X1|MT019-C02-X1]] — Trend followers split into those who resize positions continuously with volatility (and keep selling after a vol spike) and those who hold position size fixed after entry, so their flows and results differ in volatility shocks.
- [[MT019 - Backtesting Can't Predict The Future - Systematic Investor 310#^MT019-C03-1|MT019-C03-1]] — Convergence strategies and systematic macro tend to break in turbulence, unlike trend following.
- [[MT019 - Backtesting Can't Predict The Future - Systematic Investor 310#^MT019-C03-5|MT019-C03-5]] — Dampening volatility via volatility adjustment is like an aircraft stabiliser.
- [[MT019 - Backtesting Can't Predict The Future - Systematic Investor 310#^MT019-C04-3|MT019-C04-3]] — True diversification into commodities matters; managers concentrated in financials (to hold large AUM) suffered more in the month.
- [[MT019 - Backtesting Can't Predict The Future - Systematic Investor 310#^MT019-C13-1|MT019-C13-1]] — Cocoa 2024 example: trend followers who size dynamically sold 80-90% of notional Jan-May as volatility rose.
- [[MT019 - Backtesting Can't Predict The Future - Systematic Investor 310#^MT019-C14-2|MT019-C14-2]] — Conventional wisdom that highly correlated markets add no diversification is correct for most models but not for 'outlier hunter' trend followers using ensembles of models.
- [[MT019 - Backtesting Can't Predict The Future - Systematic Investor 310#^MT019-C14-3|MT019-C14-3]] — What matters is the correlation of strategy returns from trading each market, not price correlations.
- [[MT019 - Backtesting Can't Predict The Future - Systematic Investor 310#^MT019-C14-4|MT019-C14-4]] — Applying the same ensemble to Brent and WTI gives quite uncorrelated return streams despite highly correlated prices.
- [[MT019 - Backtesting Can't Predict The Future - Systematic Investor 310#^MT019-C15-1|MT019-C15-1]] — Outliers are volatile and need an ensemble of models to maximize extraction, since a single system is snagged by the volatility.
- [[MT019 - Backtesting Can't Predict The Future - Systematic Investor 310#^MT019-C15-3|MT019-C15-3]] — An outlier hunter holds and pyramids a strong trend (cocoa 2024) and accepts the big give-back when it turns.

## [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10]]
- [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10#^MT020-C01-2|MT020-C01-2]] — Trading success needs more than entry rules: risk management and attitudes toward profit and loss; Dennis ran the Turtles' risk control while Eckhardt developed rules.
- [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10#^MT020-C03-2|MT020-C03-2]] — Markets are not random but too close to random for simple predictions, so focus should be on risk control, which one can know and control, rather than prediction.
- [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10#^MT020-C04-1|MT020-C04-1]] — Do not predict the market; decide what to do wherever it goes: risk control first, with profitability following from correct risk control.
- [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10#^MT020-C04-2|MT020-C04-2]] — Utility theory: marginal utility of money diminishes, so losses weigh more than gains and risk aversion is a rigorous concept; ETC uses a small class of utility functions suited to commodity trading and client circumstances.
- [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10#^MT020-C04-3|MT020-C04-3]] — Most traders build a profitable system first and graft risk control on afterwards; ETC brings expected utility in at the ground floor of research and judges every idea by its effect on expected utility.
- [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10#^MT020-C04-4|MT020-C04-4]] — Risk-neutral and risk-loving utility functions lead to ruin (the St Petersburg paradox shows absurd infinite expected value bets).
- [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10#^MT020-C05-1|MT020-C05-1]] — In futures trading with leverage, risk aversion is primary since risk-loving traders repeatedly lose all their money.
- [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10#^MT020-C06-2|MT020-C06-2]] — Volatility enters ETC's systems in five or six ways (position sizing, measuring price change, risk control) alongside price, so the systems are based on both.
- [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10#^MT020-C06-3|MT020-C06-3]] — ETC does not target portfolio volatility because higher volatility often helps trend systems and they do not want to juice up when volatility is low; they track individual futures' volatility for risk.
- [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10#^MT020-C06-4|MT020-C06-4]] — Position sizes are kept roughly constant from entry to exit: readjusting to rising volatility would cut the position along with the trend, which they found counterproductive.
- [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10#^MT020-C07-1|MT020-C07-1]] — Constant sizing with a stop-loss gives controlled risk and a synthetic long-optionality (volatility-seeking) profile.
- [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10#^MT020-C07-X1|MT020-C07-X1]] — Resizing an open trend position to rising volatility amounts to a hidden mean-reversion trade against the trend, because volatility usually rises as the trend extends.
- [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10#^MT020-C10-2|MT020-C10-2]] — More markets give more independent bets and less noise in any run, though smaller portfolios can outperform for periods because of concentration.
- [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10#^MT020-C10-3|MT020-C10-3]] — ETC trades about 70 markets, believes in diversification as the greatest financial idea of the 20th century, but avoids markets with too much slippage or too little liquidity.
- [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10#^MT020-C11-1|MT020-C11-1]] — Alternative markets look better in backtests but slippage and exit difficulty in a hurry make them worse in practice.
- [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10#^MT020-C12-3|MT020-C12-3]] — LTCM failed because it relied on normal distribution theory despite fat tails.
- [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10#^MT020-C12-X3|MT020-C12-X3]] — Computing volatility as a standard deviation for sizing reintroduces normal-distribution assumptions and their drawbacks; range-based measures like ATR are not standard deviations.
- [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10#^MT020-C13-1|MT020-C13-1]] — Replication portfolios lack predefined exits, exposing them to discontinuity and gap events; stops and exit rules keeping losses small are essential.
- [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10#^MT020-C13-2|MT020-C13-2]] — All futures markets have heavy left tails (leptokurtosis), so trading rules must intervene to control the left tail via risk management.
- [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10#^MT020-C13-4|MT020-C13-4]] — Predictive accuracy is about 51-52%, so put ten times as much emphasis on risk control as on prediction.

## [[MT021 - Bonds Behaving Badly - Systematic Investor 309]]
- [[MT021 - Bonds Behaving Badly - Systematic Investor 309#^MT021-C07-2|MT021-C07-2]] — Trend portfolios (about 85% in fixed income) can go long or short bonds, unlike buy-and-hold, which remains exposed to valuation changes whatever yield is locked in.
- [[MT021 - Bonds Behaving Badly - Systematic Investor 309#^MT021-C07-5|MT021-C07-5]] — A trend fund holding spare cash in short-dated paper earns yield without taking the reaction-pattern risk an outright long-duration bond buyer takes.
- [[MT021 - Bonds Behaving Badly - Systematic Investor 309#^MT021-C09-1|MT021-C09-1]] — Long-short trend in bonds, the dollar and gold had negative correlation with equities in equity-down periods, and commodities went from strongly positive to flat, which diversifies a 60/40.
- [[MT021 - Bonds Behaving Badly - Systematic Investor 309#^MT021-C09-2|MT021-C09-2]] — Portfolio diversification depends on correlation and added volatility: long-only bonds raise correlation and volatility, while vol-managed assets with negative down-period correlation diversify more.
- [[MT021 - Bonds Behaving Badly - Systematic Investor 309#^MT021-C10-2|MT021-C10-2]] — A static 60/40 cannot adapt to environments where moves are not all long, whereas a dynamic long/short strategy can, and long-run averages hide decade-long gaps.
- [[MT021 - Bonds Behaving Badly - Systematic Investor 309#^MT021-C11-2|MT021-C11-2]] — First responders (short-term trading, options) profit when the VIX spikes but are hard to capture because they must be on the right side at the start.
- [[MT021 - Bonds Behaving Badly - Systematic Investor 309#^MT021-C11-4|MT021-C11-4]] — Even if prices revert, a vol spike changes volatility-targeted position sizes in trend programs, so a quick correction can still affect positions.
- [[MT021 - Bonds Behaving Badly - Systematic Investor 309#^MT021-C11-X4|MT021-C11-X4]] — Catching one-day extreme moves requires a strategy that trades that way every day, bearing its costs and losses on all the ordinary days, so such moves are very hard to capitalise on in practice.

## [[MT022 - Bonds- The Voldemort of Asset Allocation - Systematic Investor 320]]
- [[MT022 - Bonds- The Voldemort of Asset Allocation - Systematic Investor 320#^MT022-C03-3|MT022-C03-3]] — Trend followers cut losing positions without attachment, which is a virtue but means they get hurt by head fakes when the market quickly reverses back.
- [[MT022 - Bonds- The Voldemort of Asset Allocation - Systematic Investor 320#^MT022-C12-X10|MT022-C12-X10]] — The usual argument (put by the host) is that a fund combining trend with other strategies and more markets should, all else equal, have a higher Sharpe than a pure-trend ETF; Beer disputes that complexity adds much Sharpe.
- [[MT022 - Bonds- The Voldemort of Asset Allocation - Systematic Investor 320#^MT022-C12-X9|MT022-C12-X9]] — Since AlphaSimplex launched its managed futures ETF, the SG CTA trend index fell about 11% while the ETF fell about 4%, evidence that an ETF need not lack risk controls.
- [[MT022 - Bonds- The Voldemort of Asset Allocation - Systematic Investor 320#^MT022-C13-1|MT022-C13-1]] — Man's mutual fund is designed as a diversified single line item with tighter risk controls to do better at inflection points, versus a wilder ETF.
- [[MT022 - Bonds- The Voldemort of Asset Allocation - Systematic Investor 320#^MT022-C14-1|MT022-C14-1]] — In 2022 the SG CTA Index rose about 20-26% while mutual funds after 170 bp fees rose about 14%, ten points lost to controls; other periods nearly identical, about 200 bp a year over five years.
- [[MT022 - Bonds- The Voldemort of Asset Allocation - Systematic Investor 320#^MT022-C14-X11|MT022-C14-X11]] — Replicating pre-incentive-fee returns makes the product more volatile than net-of-fee CTA indices, which Beer argues investors can accept up to a point in exchange for the higher return.
- [[MT022 - Bonds- The Voldemort of Asset Allocation - Systematic Investor 320#^MT022-C18-6|MT022-C18-6]] — Stacking capital-efficient overlays on top of other assets risks unexpectedly large drawdowns when the legs fall together, and investors' drawdown tolerance is less than they say.

## [[MT023 - Building A Hedge Fund Allocation - Systematic Investor 305]]
- [[MT023 - Building A Hedge Fund Allocation - Systematic Investor 305#^MT023-C12-4|MT023-C12-4]] — Convexity comes from straddles or from delta-replicating them, which is fast trend following; the paper compares fast, medium and slow trend on skewness and return.
- [[MT023 - Building A Hedge Fund Allocation - Systematic Investor 305#^MT023-C12-5|MT023-C12-5]] — Negatively skewed strategies (equity-like) gain often with a left tail, whereas positive skew gives outsized gains in equity sell-offs, so it matters for risk mitigation.
- [[MT023 - Building A Hedge Fund Allocation - Systematic Investor 305#^MT023-C13-1|MT023-C13-1]] — Fast trend and straddles are positively skewed, slow trend is negatively skewed in daily returns, flat in monthly and positive in quarterly.
- [[MT023 - Building A Hedge Fund Allocation - Systematic Investor 305#^MT023-C13-2|MT023-C13-2]] — Trend speed is another lever beside adding non-trend: slower trend smooths returns but reduces reactivity.
- [[MT023 - Building A Hedge Fund Allocation - Systematic Investor 305#^MT023-C13-3|MT023-C13-3]] — Adding trend plus commodity carry to equities beats trend alone in skewness and return.
- [[MT023 - Building A Hedge Fund Allocation - Systematic Investor 305#^MT023-C14-1|MT023-C14-1]] — Man AHL research after the SVB event: the sharpest trend drawdown weeks tend to occur in periods of strong trends and high volatility when trend is making most money.
- [[MT023 - Building A Hedge Fund Allocation - Systematic Investor 305#^MT023-C15-3|MT023-C15-3]] — In equity declines managed futures preserved capital and showed good alpha, whereas relative value, credit and event driven did not.
- [[MT023 - Building A Hedge Fund Allocation - Systematic Investor 305#^MT023-C16-1|MT023-C16-1]] — A standalone hedge fund portfolio was about 59% loss mitigation, 19% equity diversifier and 22% equity complement, but inside a 60/40 re-optimization it goes 100% loss mitigation and allows more equity leverage.
- [[MT023 - Building A Hedge Fund Allocation - Systematic Investor 305#^MT023-C16-2|MT023-C16-2]] — Get equity beta from equities and use hedge funds for diversification.
- [[MT023 - Building A Hedge Fund Allocation - Systematic Investor 305#^MT023-C16-4|MT023-C16-4]] — The optimal number of hedge fund managers is 15-20; more is justified for tactical reasons.
- [[MT023 - Building A Hedge Fund Allocation - Systematic Investor 305#^MT023-C17-3|MT023-C17-3]] — Building a hedge fund portfolio on diversification alone (convergent plus divergent) forgets that it should be built in the context of the overall portfolio.

## [[MT024 - Building an Inflation-Proof Portfolio - Systematic Investor - Ep.384]]
- [[MT024 - Building an Inflation-Proof Portfolio - Systematic Investor - Ep.384#^MT024-C02-1|MT024-C02-1]] — Given geopolitical breakdown (Greenland, loss of trust), investors should build robust portfolios that get through the next five to seven years safely rather than maximise return.
- [[MT024 - Building an Inflation-Proof Portfolio - Systematic Investor - Ep.384#^MT024-C02-2|MT024-C02-2]] — Robustness comes only from combining truly uncorrelated strategies and assets.
- [[MT024 - Building an Inflation-Proof Portfolio - Systematic Investor - Ep.384#^MT024-C06-4|MT024-C06-4]] — A 50% bonds, 50% commodity trend portfolio gives roughly CPI+4% in every inflation environment with little equity correlation, so no inflation forecast is needed.
- [[MT024 - Building an Inflation-Proof Portfolio - Systematic Investor - Ep.384#^MT024-C06-5|MT024-C06-5]] — CTAs, especially commodity CTAs, can be framed as part of a defensive, all-weather building block with bonds rather than only as an equity diversifier with convexity.
- [[MT024 - Building an Inflation-Proof Portfolio - Systematic Investor - Ep.384#^MT024-C08-3|MT024-C08-3]] — Bonds win if inflation falls and commodity trend wins if inflation rises, so the mix is resilient without forecasting inflation.
- [[MT024 - Building an Inflation-Proof Portfolio - Systematic Investor - Ep.384#^MT024-C10-3|MT024-C10-3]] — CTAs were long cocoa from early 2023 and sold into the 2024 rally through risk management, so they did not cause the spike.

## [[MT026 - CTA Performance- Less is More- - Systematic Investor 319]]
- [[MT026 - CTA Performance- Less is More- - Systematic Investor 319#^MT026-C02-3|MT026-C02-3]] — Volatility-targeted trend followers cut position size as volatility expands, so a big rally such as cocoa in 2024 is accompanied by CTA selling of position size, the opposite of the claim that CTAs pushed the price up.
- [[MT026 - CTA Performance- Less is More- - Systematic Investor 319#^MT026-C07-2|MT026-C07-2]] — A slow system's desired position barely changes if the latest price moves, so intraday and overnight price movements between signal and execution can be ignored on average.
- [[MT026 - CTA Performance- Less is More- - Systematic Investor 319#^MT026-C08-1|MT026-C08-1]] — Dynamic optimisation compares current positions with the zero-cost ideal portfolio and trades only when moving is worth the cost, sometimes not trading at all or trading in cheaper markets.
- [[MT026 - CTA Performance- Less is More- - Systematic Investor 319#^MT026-C08-2|MT026-C08-2]] — A simple buffer skips a trade if the position changes by less than 10% of a standard vol-adjusted position unit.
- [[MT026 - CTA Performance- Less is More- - Systematic Investor 319#^MT026-C08-X1|MT026-C08-X1]] — A dynamic-optimisation system could be rerun hourly to pick up new prices (handling stale prices in closed markets), but for a slow system trading off the prior close is good enough and Rob does not bother.
- [[MT026 - CTA Performance- Less is More- - Systematic Investor 319#^MT026-C09-2|MT026-C09-2]] — A long call automatically pyramids as price rises because delta increases, mimicking trend followers' increasing exposure.
- [[MT026 - CTA Performance- Less is More- - Systematic Investor 319#^MT026-C09-3|MT026-C09-3]] — Choose between futures and options for a directional view by comparing implied vol with forecast vol: buy calls when implied is below forecast, use futures when implied is high.
- [[MT026 - CTA Performance- Less is More- - Systematic Investor 319#^MT026-C10-1|MT026-C10-1]] — With zero costs one should always rebalance; as transaction costs rise, the optimal tolerance for deviation from the target position widens.
- [[MT026 - CTA Performance- Less is More- - Systematic Investor 319#^MT026-C10-2|MT026-C10-2]] — A portfolio-level cost-aware optimiser can express a required bond exposure through the cheapest-to-trade correlated market and leave expensive positions untouched.
- [[MT026 - CTA Performance- Less is More- - Systematic Investor 319#^MT026-C10-3|MT026-C10-3]] — A retail trader with 25% risk target pays about 1% a year in costs (half commissions, half spreads); an institution would pay about 60 bp (50 bp spread, 10 bp commission).
- [[MT026 - CTA Performance- Less is More- - Systematic Investor 319#^MT026-C11-1|MT026-C11-1]] — In theory the position-inertia buffer should be wider for expensive instruments and narrower for cheap ones; the fixed 10% is a simplification that suits retail single-lot trades.
- [[MT026 - CTA Performance- Less is More- - Systematic Investor 319#^MT026-C11-2|MT026-C11-2]] — For measuring current portfolio risk, estimate correlations between instrument returns with an exponentially weighted window of about 6 months, balancing responsiveness against noise.
- [[MT026 - CTA Performance- Less is More- - Systematic Investor 319#^MT026-C12-3|MT026-C12-3]] — Correlations between trading-strategy returns (e.g. trend on Bund vs trend on US 10y) are lower and more stable than between assets, so estimate them over 10 or more years covering more than one cycle; exponential weighting matters little with that window.
- [[MT026 - CTA Performance- Less is More- - Systematic Investor 319#^MT026-C12-4|MT026-C12-4]] — Automated monitoring: a status web page refreshed every 10 minutes shows whether processes are running, crashed or stale, plus nightly emails confirming normal operation.
- [[MT026 - CTA Performance- Less is More- - Systematic Investor 319#^MT026-C13-1|MT026-C13-1]] — Automated checks flag missing prices for several days, missing daily positions, and differences between broker and system positions (often from expired futures) so human supervision is minimal.
- [[MT026 - CTA Performance- Less is More- - Systematic Investor 319#^MT026-C16-3|MT026-C16-3]] — Adding many markets also adds many chances of ranging markets, so more markets are different, not necessarily better.

## [[MT027 - CTAs vs ETFs- The Showdown That Could Change Everything - Systematic Investor 339]]
- [[MT027 - CTAs vs ETFs- The Showdown That Could Change Everything - Systematic Investor 339#^MT027-C07-4|MT027-C07-4]] — Portable alpha overlays diversifiers on top of full equity exposure, so investors protect the left tail without giving up the equity right tail.
- [[MT027 - CTAs vs ETFs- The Showdown That Could Change Everything - Systematic Investor 339#^MT027-C09-1|MT027-C09-1]] — Stacked equity plus trend products suffer when both fall together (e.g. S&P -6% and trend -4% = -10% in a month), which is hard to explain to risk-averse clients for a zero long-term beta strategy.
- [[MT027 - CTAs vs ETFs- The Showdown That Could Change Everything - Systematic Investor 339#^MT027-C10-2|MT027-C10-2]] — Portable alpha lets tactical diversifier positions coexist with strategic allocation without moving dollars between asset classes.
- [[MT027 - CTAs vs ETFs- The Showdown That Could Change Everything - Systematic Investor 339#^MT027-C11-4|MT027-C11-4]] — A naive alternative to regime timing is to run several model time frames with a roughly constant allocation across them.
- [[MT027 - CTAs vs ETFs- The Showdown That Could Change Everything - Systematic Investor 339#^MT027-C12-4|MT027-C12-4]] — Faster, more diversified CTA programs (e.g. American Beacon/Man AHL) cost about 100bp more plus extra trading costs and likely lower Sharpe, despite better drawdown control and inflection performance.
- [[MT027 - CTAs vs ETFs- The Showdown That Could Change Everything - Systematic Investor 339#^MT027-C14-2|MT027-C14-2]] — Dynamic risk allocation using trend-environment indicators (e.g. Trend Barometer) can modestly improve results as long as one does not over-predict the future or trade the same way regardless of environment.
- [[MT027 - CTAs vs ETFs- The Showdown That Could Change Everything - Systematic Investor 339#^MT027-C15-3|MT027-C15-3]] — In sharp shocks vol controls make managers derisk faster than a weekly replicator (e.g. 100 to 70 vs replicator 180 to 100), but the industry never flips fully, so residual lag is bounded.
- [[MT027 - CTAs vs ETFs- The Showdown That Could Change Everything - Systematic Investor 339#^MT027-C15-X2|MT027-C15-X2]] — Vol controls and stops de-risk only after the loss has occurred, so a weekly replicator's slower de-risking costs money only if the move keeps going after the shock; if it reverses, being slow does no harm.
- [[MT027 - CTAs vs ETFs- The Showdown That Could Change Everything - Systematic Investor 339#^MT027-C16-1|MT027-C16-1]] — Sharp short-lived volatility spikes (three-day wall spikes, like the yen carry unwind) trigger false vol-control signals and cut risk at the wrong time, adding volatility without clearly hurting long-term Sharpe.

## [[MT028 - CTAs- Innovation Meets Legacy & the BlackRock Effect - Systematic Investor 329]]
- [[MT028 - CTAs- Innovation Meets Legacy & the BlackRock Effect - Systematic Investor 329#^MT028-C09-4|MT028-C09-4]] — Large CTAs cap sizes in illiquid idiosyncratic markets, so single markets like cocoa contribute little to their results.
- [[MT028 - CTAs- Innovation Meets Legacy & the BlackRock Effect - Systematic Investor 329#^MT028-C10-5|MT028-C10-5]] — Concentrating on idiosyncratic high-asymmetry bets raises risk and drawdowns and is hard to model ex ante, so broad factor exposure is more reliable.
- [[MT028 - CTAs- Innovation Meets Legacy & the BlackRock Effect - Systematic Investor 329#^MT028-C11-2|MT028-C11-2]] — Trend Sharpe of a single market (0.2-0.3) rises with more markets, and many CTAs reach roughly 0.9 only by adding many idiosyncratic bets, which a 10-instrument replicator cannot access.
- [[MT028 - CTAs- Innovation Meets Legacy & the BlackRock Effect - Systematic Investor 329#^MT028-C13-2|MT028-C13-2]] — Trend followers make only small tweaks to models (few ways to do trend following); improvement over the last decade is mainly in risk management and execution.
- [[MT028 - CTAs- Innovation Meets Legacy & the BlackRock Effect - Systematic Investor 329#^MT028-C14-2|MT028-C14-2]] — Trend models are standard off-the-shelf (e.g. moving-average crossover); the edge lies in implementation, risk allocation and recognition of over-extended trends, with risk management and execution the main innovation areas.
- [[MT028 - CTAs- Innovation Meets Legacy & the BlackRock Effect - Systematic Investor 329#^MT028-C17-1|MT028-C17-1]] — Portable alpha, overlaying CTAs on top of equity exposure (60/40/20), is rising because margin-based strategies are cheap to implement.

## [[MT029 - CTAs- The Good, The Bad, & The Misunderstood - Top Traders Unplugged 148]]
- [[MT029 - CTAs- The Good, The Bad, & The Misunderstood - Top Traders Unplugged 148#^MT029-C05-X1|MT029-C05-X1]] — Crypto has enormous right skew, so risk control and position management for trend in crypto should differ from other asset classes; a generalist implementation leaves alpha on the table.
- [[MT029 - CTAs- The Good, The Bad, & The Misunderstood - Top Traders Unplugged 148#^MT029-C06-4|MT029-C06-4]] — A single universal risk model that includes higher moments can handle assets with different distributions such as crypto, so separate asset-class funds are not needed; a standalone CTA should choose the asset weights itself.
- [[MT029 - CTAs- The Good, The Bad, & The Misunderstood - Top Traders Unplugged 148#^MT029-C07-1|MT029-C07-1]] — A fixed income trend portfolio has strong left skew, like multi-strat pods, so it needs tighter risk control than naturally diversified commodity portfolios.
- [[MT029 - CTAs- The Good, The Bad, & The Misunderstood - Top Traders Unplugged 148#^MT029-C07-2|MT029-C07-2]] — Bond liquidity is fragmented across many maturities of the same issuer, so execution must tolerate partial fills or substitute bonds, unlike equities.
- [[MT029 - CTAs- The Good, The Bad, & The Misunderstood - Top Traders Unplugged 148#^MT029-C09-4|MT029-C09-4]] — Liquidity limits how much risk can go into small diversifying markets, so one must either accept small size or cap capacity to keep diversification.
- [[MT029 - CTAs- The Good, The Bad, & The Misunderstood - Top Traders Unplugged 148#^MT029-C09-5|MT029-C09-5]] — Better execution in fixed income comes from understanding who holds inventory and using many brokers rather than only big banks; systems should accept partial fills or different bonds on the same curve.
- [[MT029 - CTAs- The Good, The Bad, & The Misunderstood - Top Traders Unplugged 148#^MT029-C11-2|MT029-C11-2]] — In less liquid OTC markets you cannot exit when vol spikes as futures CTAs do, so a properly diversified portfolio must be built before the crisis.
- [[MT029 - CTAs- The Good, The Bad, & The Misunderstood - Top Traders Unplugged 148#^MT029-C12-3|MT029-C12-3]] — Speeding up a trend program would give more positive skew and tail protection but at the cost of alpha, moving away from where trend works best.
- [[MT029 - CTAs- The Good, The Bad, & The Misunderstood - Top Traders Unplugged 148#^MT029-C14-1|MT029-C14-1]] — For liquid CTAs execution costs about 5bp of Sharpe per year, so execution is production work; for illiquid markets it can cost 30-40bp of Sharpe and matters.
- [[MT029 - CTAs- The Good, The Bad, & The Misunderstood - Top Traders Unplugged 148#^MT029-C14-2|MT029-C14-2]] — Delaying signals by a day costs only 1-2bp, so execution desks should be given several days to trade patiently, using partial execution to avoid crossing the spread, which is the real cost.
- [[MT029 - CTAs- The Good, The Bad, & The Misunderstood - Top Traders Unplugged 148#^MT029-C15-1|MT029-C15-1]] — Many CTAs smooth signals for days then force execution within hours; better to give the desk the raw signal and tell it to trade slowly, saving costs.
- [[MT029 - CTAs- The Good, The Bad, & The Misunderstood - Top Traders Unplugged 148#^MT029-C15-2|MT029-C15-2]] — With trading costs of about 4bp Sharpe (half commissions), only about 2bp can be saved through execution effort on liquid futures, so effort is not worthwhile there; benefit exists mainly in OTC bonds.
- [[MT029 - CTAs- The Good, The Bad, & The Misunderstood - Top Traders Unplugged 148#^MT029-C15-X1|MT029-C15-X1]] — Yav claims patient execution saves more than the alpha lost from delay even in liquid bond futures (e.g. 5-year Bobl), not only in illiquid OTC bonds; Rob disagrees for liquid futures.
- [[MT029 - CTAs- The Good, The Bad, & The Misunderstood - Top Traders Unplugged 148#^MT029-C16-1|MT029-C16-1]] — Giving traders discretion over patient execution turns a CTA into CTA plus a short-horizon alpha desk, requiring skills that may not exist; with 30bp costs Rob would slow the signal further or stop trading the market.

## [[MT030 - Can Trend Following Be Saved- - Systematic Investor 348]]
- [[MT030 - Can Trend Following Be Saved- - Systematic Investor 348#^MT030-C08-3|MT030-C08-3]] — Rather than speeding up models, whipsaw protection may come from improved risk management.
- [[MT030 - Can Trend Following Be Saved- - Systematic Investor 348#^MT030-C12-3|MT030-C12-3]] — Measured as max drawdown relative to annualised volatility over 12 years, bonds have by far the worst ratio and equities are not better than many CTA managers, so current CTA drawdowns are not extreme in relative terms.
- [[MT030 - Can Trend Following Be Saved- - Systematic Investor 348#^MT030-C16-3|MT030-C16-3]] — Trading only 10 super-liquid contracts gives about 8bp of trading costs, whereas a competitor trading many more markets shows about 200bp, so complex programs must beat cheap ones by a wide margin.
- [[MT030 - Can Trend Following Be Saved- - Systematic Investor 348#^MT030-C18-3|MT030-C18-3]] — Managers with few markets, bigger risk in smaller markets and static position sizing had top returns over five years but outsized losses now.
- [[MT030 - Can Trend Following Be Saved- - Systematic Investor 348#^MT030-C19-1|MT030-C19-1]] — Return-stacked managed futures plus equity products lag now that CTA alpha is negative and equity beta has recovered, behaving exactly as designed.
- [[MT030 - Can Trend Following Be Saved- - Systematic Investor 348#^MT030-C20-3|MT030-C20-3]] — Managed futures should be about a 3% allocation: large enough to matter in a 2022-type event but small enough to hide in the tall grass in years like 2025.

## [[MT031 - Cem Karsan on the Market’s Quiet Fragility - Systematic Investor - Ep.379]]
- [[MT031 - Cem Karsan on the Market’s Quiet Fragility - Systematic Investor - Ep.379#^MT031-C05-4|MT031-C05-4]] — Good risk management (diversification, quality) lets you apply leverage and earn higher returns, rather than just taking less risk.
- [[MT031 - Cem Karsan on the Market’s Quiet Fragility - Systematic Investor - Ep.379#^MT031-C05-5|MT031-C05-5]] — 60/40 just lowers total risk and return at the same Sharpe ratio; bonds do not diversify stocks.
- [[MT031 - Cem Karsan on the Market’s Quiet Fragility - Systematic Investor - Ep.379#^MT031-C05-6|MT031-C05-6]] — Buffett's record includes about 1.5-2x leverage and steep drawdowns, so his returns are not just stock picking.
- [[MT031 - Cem Karsan on the Market’s Quiet Fragility - Systematic Investor - Ep.379#^MT031-C06-1|MT031-C06-1]] — Hedge funds first raise Sharpe through diversification and risk control, then use leverage to create outsize returns; without leverage Buffett-like quality only lowers risk.
- [[MT031 - Cem Karsan on the Market’s Quiet Fragility - Systematic Investor - Ep.379#^MT031-C12-1|MT031-C12-1]] — Future Fund defines portfolio resilience as exposures achieving target return across many scenarios, with diversification, shock absorption, predictability, and protection against severe losses.
- [[MT031 - Cem Karsan on the Market’s Quiet Fragility - Systematic Investor - Ep.379#^MT031-C13-1|MT031-C13-1]] — 60/40 is suboptimal without a view on rates and is dangerous if rates rise, unlevered or not.
- [[MT031 - Cem Karsan on the Market’s Quiet Fragility - Systematic Investor - Ep.379#^MT031-C13-5|MT031-C13-5]] — Diversification, a little convexity (long ball), geometric-return focus and value investing give a robust portfolio without needing alpha.
- [[MT031 - Cem Karsan on the Market’s Quiet Fragility - Systematic Investor - Ep.379#^MT031-C13-6|MT031-C13-6]] — Simple risk-management concepts could raise a 0.35 Sharpe several-fold; nobody does it because people extrapolate recent history.

## [[MT032 - China Open-Source, Compute Arms Race, Reordering Global Trade - BG2 w- Bill Gurley and Bra]]
- [[MT032 - China Open-Source, Compute Arms Race, Reordering Global Trade - BG2 w- Bill Gurley and Bra#^MT032-C10-1|MT032-C10-1]] — AI model companies price below cost for market share, with rumors of negative gross margin, so a repricing would create a bump in supply and demand when it ends.
- [[MT032 - China Open-Source, Compute Arms Race, Reordering Global Trade - BG2 w- Bill Gurley and Bra#^MT032-C11-2|MT032-C11-2]] — Share-seeking labs depend on capital markets and funding can disappear quickly, so labs without consumer products are vulnerable.

## [[MT035 - Coatue’s Laffont Brothers. AI, Public & VC Mkts, Macro, US Debt, Crypto, IPO's, & more - B]]
- [[MT035 - Coatue’s Laffont Brothers. AI, Public & VC Mkts, Macro, US Debt, Crypto, IPO's, & more - B#^MT035-C04-1|MT035-C04-1]] — Public institutions avoid marked-to-market assets with 70-80% downside, whereas private portfolios hold many unmarked bets where a few winners pay for zeros.
- [[MT035 - Coatue’s Laffont Brothers. AI, Public & VC Mkts, Macro, US Debt, Crypto, IPO's, & more - B#^MT035-C08-6|MT035-C08-6]] — Macro matters even for fundamental investors but obsessing about it can undo you.
- [[MT035 - Coatue’s Laffont Brothers. AI, Public & VC Mkts, Macro, US Debt, Crypto, IPO's, & more - B#^MT035-C15-4|MT035-C15-4]] — Growing companies that burn cash should raise large war chests now (OpenAI raised $40B).

## [[MT036 - Commodity Super Cycle Inbound! - Systematic Investor 294]]
- [[MT036 - Commodity Super Cycle Inbound! - Systematic Investor 294#^MT036-C02-1|MT036-C02-1]] — The good record of short-vol 0DTE strategies reflects a period without any major volatility spike; a spike would be the real test and he sees something brewing.
- [[MT036 - Commodity Super Cycle Inbound! - Systematic Investor 294#^MT036-C02-2|MT036-C02-2]] — Zero-day options are useful as a fixed-cost patch for tail risk in a portfolio, which is why demand and open interest at the short end soared.
- [[MT036 - Commodity Super Cycle Inbound! - Systematic Investor 294#^MT036-C02-X1|MT036-C02-X1]] — An equity bear market can unfold without a volatility spike (2022 was a down year but not very volatile), so long-volatility protection need not pay in a slow grinding decline and short-vol records since 2022 are untested.
- [[MT036 - Commodity Super Cycle Inbound! - Systematic Investor 294#^MT036-C02-X2|MT036-C02-X2]] — The April 2024 reversals (yen intervention, sharp cocoa sell-off, equity weakness) led some trend followers to cut risk.
- [[MT036 - Commodity Super Cycle Inbound! - Systematic Investor 294#^MT036-C03-3|MT036-C03-3]] — In April 2024 CTA indices rose while MSCI World fell 3.85% and world government bonds fell 1.77%, showing trend following diversified stock and bond losses.
- [[MT036 - Commodity Super Cycle Inbound! - Systematic Investor 294#^MT036-C04-2|MT036-C04-2]] — Volatility-scaled position sizing makes trend followers reduce positions as volatility rises, so they were net sellers of cocoa during its 2024 spike.
- [[MT036 - Commodity Super Cycle Inbound! - Systematic Investor 294#^MT036-C07-2|MT036-C07-2]] — In hedging, forward-curve roll-down can pay for option time decay (rho pays for theta) when rates are below the dividend yield.
- [[MT036 - Commodity Super Cycle Inbound! - Systematic Investor 294#^MT036-C07-3|MT036-C07-3]] — In commodities the structure is reversed: option time decay (theta) earned from selling rich options pays for the futures roll-down.
- [[MT036 - Commodity Super Cycle Inbound! - Systematic Investor 294#^MT036-C07-7|MT036-C07-7]] — Selling moderately OTM calls while buying further OTM calls and covering the extreme is attractive and funds the carry of long futures.
- [[MT036 - Commodity Super Cycle Inbound! - Systematic Investor 294#^MT036-C07-8|MT036-C07-8]] — Virtual warehousing buys futures at the cheapest curve points and funds roll costs from rich option premium so that holding a flat market costs close to zero.
- [[MT036 - Commodity Super Cycle Inbound! - Systematic Investor 294#^MT036-C08-4|MT036-C08-4]] — Trend following has built-in risk control: the position is held while the trend lasts and exits when it breaks.
- [[MT036 - Commodity Super Cycle Inbound! - Systematic Investor 294#^MT036-C09-1|MT036-C09-1]] — The warehousing structure loses ~25% of further declines and captures ~2/3 of rallies (only ~50% of a one-day spike), a rule-of-thumb payoff.
- [[MT036 - Commodity Super Cycle Inbound! - Systematic Investor 294#^MT036-C09-2|MT036-C09-2]] — If spot is flat the long futures drift down but the combined structure stays flat because option premium offsets roll costs.
- [[MT036 - Commodity Super Cycle Inbound! - Systematic Investor 294#^MT036-C10-4|MT036-C10-4]] — The structure is bounded: sell moderate OTM options, buy deep OTM and at-the-money options so all upside is covered.
- [[MT036 - Commodity Super Cycle Inbound! - Systematic Investor 294#^MT036-C10-5|MT036-C10-5]] — In hedging, buy volatility when cheap and switch to trend following for downside protection once vol is expensive.
- [[MT036 - Commodity Super Cycle Inbound! - Systematic Investor 294#^MT036-C10-8|MT036-C10-8]] — Every option structure encodes a regime bet, so participation should be regime-based.
- [[MT036 - Commodity Super Cycle Inbound! - Systematic Investor 294#^MT036-C10-X1|MT036-C10-X1]] — Trend followers allocate equal risk across a fixed, broad universe because they cannot predict where the next big trend will appear.
- [[MT036 - Commodity Super Cycle Inbound! - Systematic Investor 294#^MT036-C12-2|MT036-C12-2]] — The strategy is systematic: each regime (trend- or vol-based) maps onto a standard trade structure.
- [[MT036 - Commodity Super Cycle Inbound! - Systematic Investor 294#^MT036-C12-3|MT036-C12-3]] — Execution is manual: legging into trades, choosing time of day and sourcing liquidity.

## [[MT037 - Crafting the Perfect Investment Portfolio in 2025 - Systematic Investor 331]]
- [[MT037 - Crafting the Perfect Investment Portfolio in 2025 - Systematic Investor 331#^MT037-C04-1|MT037-C04-1]] — Portfolios are built around risks investors have thought of, while the real risk is vulnerability to events they have not considered, so unknown-unknown exposure should be addressed.
- [[MT037 - Crafting the Perfect Investment Portfolio in 2025 - Systematic Investor 331#^MT037-C04-3|MT037-C04-3]] — Investors treat private assets as diversifiers although they hold similar risk, an illusion of diversification.
- [[MT037 - Crafting the Perfect Investment Portfolio in 2025 - Systematic Investor 331#^MT037-C12-4|MT037-C12-4]] — Trend following in equities protects the downside and keeps investors invested, reducing the cost of too much caution.
- [[MT037 - Crafting the Perfect Investment Portfolio in 2025 - Systematic Investor 331#^MT037-C13-2|MT037-C13-2]] — Leverage constraints stop retail and private-wealth investors from levering the optimal portfolio.
- [[MT037 - Crafting the Perfect Investment Portfolio in 2025 - Systematic Investor 331#^MT037-C13-3|MT037-C13-3]] — Liquidity needs restrict illiquid allocations.
- [[MT037 - Crafting the Perfect Investment Portfolio in 2025 - Systematic Investor 331#^MT037-C13-4|MT037-C13-4]] — Combining uncorrelated positive-return strategies such as trend following with equities improves Sharpe, yet many do not allocate.
- [[MT037 - Crafting the Perfect Investment Portfolio in 2025 - Systematic Investor 331#^MT037-C15-2|MT037-C15-2]] — The composition of an alternatives allocation (liquid vs illiquid) matters more than the headline percentage.
- [[MT037 - Crafting the Perfect Investment Portfolio in 2025 - Systematic Investor 331#^MT037-C15-3|MT037-C15-3]] — If borrowing is not possible, investors can lever by choosing higher-volatility managers.
- [[MT037 - Crafting the Perfect Investment Portfolio in 2025 - Systematic Investor 331#^MT037-C15-6|MT037-C15-6]] — Given uncertainty about true Sharpe ratios, blend several diversifying strategies.
- [[MT037 - Crafting the Perfect Investment Portfolio in 2025 - Systematic Investor 331#^MT037-C16-1|MT037-C16-1]] — Trend following and risk mitigation allow meaningful equity exposure by cutting losses, instead of low-risk diversification that slows returns.
- [[MT037 - Crafting the Perfect Investment Portfolio in 2025 - Systematic Investor 331#^MT037-C16-2|MT037-C16-2]] — Bond-heavy allocations (60/40 or 80/20 in bonds, as in parts of Europe) are devastated in inflationary regimes.
- [[MT037 - Crafting the Perfect Investment Portfolio in 2025 - Systematic Investor 331#^MT037-C16-3|MT037-C16-3]] — Institutional portfolios of public equity, PE and private credit load on one equity factor, so the apparent diversification is illusory.

## [[MT038 - Crisis Alpha Is Not What Most Investors Think - Systematic Investor - Ep.400]]
- [[MT038 - Crisis Alpha Is Not What Most Investors Think - Systematic Investor - Ep.400#^MT038-C06-3|MT038-C06-3]] — BAB is built by going long low-beta and short high-beta assets, scaled to be beta neutral, which makes it zero beta by construction.
- [[MT038 - Crisis Alpha Is Not What Most Investors Think - Systematic Investor - Ep.400#^MT038-C07-1|MT038-C07-1]] — To be beta neutral BAB needs about $2 of low-beta stock per $1 of short high-beta, so it is net long notional.
- [[MT038 - Crisis Alpha Is Not What Most Investors Think - Systematic Investor - Ep.400#^MT038-C08-3|MT038-C08-3]] — BAB is always net long notional so it earns the equity drift from a long equity bull market, while having low correlation to equity.
- [[MT038 - Crisis Alpha Is Not What Most Investors Think - Systematic Investor - Ep.400#^MT038-C09-4|MT038-C09-4]] — A trend portfolio can be tilted and still defensive if the tilts are beta-neutral within each asset class.
