- [[MT201 - Trend Following...Austrian Style - Open Interest 8#^MT201-C09-3|MT201-C09-3]] — Correlation spreads between related commodities (New York versus London cocoa, wheat types) are traded as synthetic markets with their own trends; synthetics are roughly 20-25% of risk.
- [[MT201 - Trend Following...Austrian Style - Open Interest 8#^MT201-C09-X1|MT201-C09-X1]] — Breaking equity index futures into sectors or single names would add diversification to a trend portfolio, but SMN has not done it because it would need derivatives (counterparty risk) or a change of the fund prospectus.
- [[MT201 - Trend Following...Austrian Style - Open Interest 8#^MT201-C10-3|MT201-C10-3]] — VIX positions are monitored on hourly snapshots of the curve with quick intraday exits because volatility can explode.

## [[MT202 - Trump 2.0 & The Unpinning of Volatility - Systematic Investor 307]]
- [[MT202 - Trump 2.0 & The Unpinning of Volatility - Systematic Investor 307#^MT202-C05-1|MT202-C05-1]] — Mechanically buying the same puts loses money over the long run since options are insurance, but the case for it is geometric return benefit under rebalancing.
- [[MT202 - Trump 2.0 & The Unpinning of Volatility - Systematic Investor 307#^MT202-C05-2|MT202-C05-2]] — Put payoffs in market falls can be monetised and reinvested via rebalancing, turning a negative standalone return into positive portfolio geometric growth.
- [[MT202 - Trump 2.0 & The Unpinning of Volatility - Systematic Investor 307#^MT202-C06-2|MT202-C06-2]] — A volatility allocation has modest standalone return but a large geometric-return benefit at portfolio level, so it should be a systematic part of the portfolio.
- [[MT202 - Trump 2.0 & The Unpinning of Volatility - Systematic Investor 307#^MT202-C07-X1|MT202-C07-X1]] — A leveraged carry position with a fat left tail must be paired with convexity elsewhere in the portfolio, because linear hedges are an imperfect and crowded offset.
- [[MT202 - Trump 2.0 & The Unpinning of Volatility - Systematic Investor 307#^MT202-C16-X2|MT202-C16-X2]] — Jim's concrete hedge for the rotation, recommended for about three weeks before the break, was puts on Nvidia, the most crowded winner, rather than index puts.

## [[MT203 - Trump's NEW Treasury Secretary on Macro Strategy ft. Scott Bessent]]
- [[MT203 - Trump's NEW Treasury Secretary on Macro Strategy ft. Scott Bessent#^MT203-C05-1|MT203-C05-1]] — Great trades have asymmetric payoffs: small negative carry with large upside (Paulson subprime CDS: lose up to 25%, make 250-350%).
- [[MT203 - Trump's NEW Treasury Secretary on Macro Strategy ft. Scott Bessent#^MT203-C12-1|MT203-C12-1]] — FTX lacked custody separation and risk management and was exposed to a run like a 1907 bank without a lender of last resort; a counterparty that dismisses basic questions should be avoided.

## [[MT204 - Unpacking QIS- Structure, Scale, and Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.372]]
- [[MT204 - Unpacking QIS- Structure, Scale, and Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.372#^MT204-C04-2|MT204-C04-2]] — Exits go to flat (neutral zone with no-trade area) rather than reversing into a short, so after a stop-out the system waits for a new trend signal.
- [[MT204 - Unpacking QIS- Structure, Scale, and Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.372#^MT204-C10-2|MT204-C10-2]] — Equity momentum is vol-targeted, which reduces its negative skew and makes option pricing on top easier.
- [[MT204 - Unpacking QIS- Structure, Scale, and Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.372#^MT204-C11-1|MT204-C11-1]] — Volatility targeting hurts strategies that recover after volatility spikes (rebounds), because it cuts exposure when the rebound starts, so options-friendly vol-targeting can be suboptimal.
- [[MT204 - Unpacking QIS- Structure, Scale, and Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.372#^MT204-C11-3|MT204-C11-3]] — Banks can close an index when a design can no longer be run without market impact, unlike an ETF.

## [[MT205 - Unpacking the Chaos- What Today’s Market Signals Really Mean - Systematic Investor 343]]
- [[MT205 - Unpacking the Chaos- What Today’s Market Signals Really Mean - Systematic Investor 343#^MT205-C03-3|MT205-C03-3]] — Safe-haven assets need rethinking: gold held up, crypto would have been unsafe, and trend following may be a safe-haven because it adapts.
- [[MT205 - Unpacking the Chaos- What Today’s Market Signals Really Mean - Systematic Investor 343#^MT205-C05-4|MT205-C05-4]] — Volatility explosion cuts position sizes even faster than trend reversal signals do.
- [[MT205 - Unpacking the Chaos- What Today’s Market Signals Really Mean - Systematic Investor 343#^MT205-C08-1|MT205-C08-1]] — Trend followers have positive convexity over months but negative skew day-to-day in each asset class, so a day-one cross-asset shock cannot be adjusted away.
- [[MT205 - Unpacking the Chaos- What Today’s Market Signals Really Mean - Systematic Investor 343#^MT205-C08-2|MT205-C08-2]] — In an across-the-board crisis (oil, equities, FX and bonds all moving) market count does not diversify; rigorous risk management on day one is the defence.
- [[MT205 - Unpacking the Chaos- What Today’s Market Signals Really Mean - Systematic Investor 343#^MT205-C08-3|MT205-C08-3]] — Commodity trend markets are the most consistent performers in equity crises, so large commodity weight is valuable (gold, metals okay, energy strong in April).
- [[MT205 - Unpacking the Chaos- What Today’s Market Signals Really Mean - Systematic Investor 343#^MT205-C09-1|MT205-C09-1]] — Market count matters little; diversification for CTAs depends on correlation structure and overweighting less correlated assets (per the Quantica paper), not on the square root of markets.
- [[MT205 - Unpacking the Chaos- What Today’s Market Signals Really Mean - Systematic Investor 343#^MT205-C09-2|MT205-C09-2]] — A sudden rise in cross-market correlation in a crisis defeats presumed diversification and hurts CTAs.
- [[MT205 - Unpacking the Chaos- What Today’s Market Signals Really Mean - Systematic Investor 343#^MT205-C09-3|MT205-C09-3]] — Trading-cost improvements are worth about 5 bp of Sharpe per year, so reducing market impact (mixing signals, trading the second contract rather than the first) is useful but small.
- [[MT205 - Unpacking the Chaos- What Today’s Market Signals Really Mean - Systematic Investor 343#^MT205-C12-1|MT205-C12-1]] — CTA diversification is the volatility reduction factor (diversification factor 3 turns average market Sharpe 0.25 into 0.75), not the number of markets.
- [[MT205 - Unpacking the Chaos- What Today’s Market Signals Really Mean - Systematic Investor 343#^MT205-C12-2|MT205-C12-2]] — Diversification is dominated by the average market beta to the first principal factor, not by the square root of N: with 100 stocks at 30% beta, index risk is $30 versus under $10 idiosyncratic.
- [[MT205 - Unpacking the Chaos- What Today’s Market Signals Really Mean - Systematic Investor 343#^MT205-C12-3|MT205-C12-3]] — Capacity constraints can be extra alpha: smaller managers can trade low-correlation markets (commodities) at meaningful size, which large managers cannot.
- [[MT205 - Unpacking the Chaos- What Today’s Market Signals Really Mean - Systematic Investor 343#^MT205-C13-1|MT205-C13-1]] — Allocating meaningfully to low-beta markets (metals, some energy) lowers the portfolio's beta to the first factor and improves quality of diversification.
- [[MT205 - Unpacking the Chaos- What Today’s Market Signals Really Mean - Systematic Investor 343#^MT205-C14-2|MT205-C14-2]] — Trading off capacity for off-the-beaten-path markets improves diversification and reduces correlation to the CTA index and peers.
- [[MT205 - Unpacking the Chaos- What Today’s Market Signals Really Mean - Systematic Investor 343#^MT205-C17-1|MT205-C17-1]] — Do not trade a short-term signal spike that will revert before costs are recouped, especially in expensive markets; awareness of expected signal path improves cost control.

## [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315]]
- [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315#^MT206-C05-2|MT206-C05-2]] — The flow-driven rally has a fat left tail: a 20% fall around the election would remove the year-end reinvestment flows and could accelerate the decline.
- [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315#^MT206-C07-3|MT206-C07-3]] — Cham sees the China stimulus rally as a tactical trade into December-January, best expressed with calls and limited downside, not as an investment.

## [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364]]
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C05-1|MT207-C05-1]] — Portfolio construction goal: as many truly independent return streams as possible (cocoa vs S&P, sugar vs OJ, California carbon vs 10-year), not a target market count.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C05-2|MT207-C05-2]] — Casino analogy: a small trend edge applied across many independent bets raises expected risk-adjusted return.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C05-3|MT207-C05-3]] — Small diversifying markets (Chinese futures, canola, lumber, livestock, carbon) cannot absorb big capital, so capacity is capped around $500 million to keep a meaningful footprint.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C06-2|MT207-C06-2]] — Brokered futures markets (CCAs, South African grains) cost a penny more but may give better execution than hitting screen quotes, so they are not 'crappy' markets.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C06-X1|MT207-C06-X1]] — Market selection is limited by access and operational complexity: very small emerging carbon markets (e.g. New Zealand) are excluded because setting them up is time-intensive and adds operational risk, not because of weak trend properties.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C07-2|MT207-C07-2]] — London vs New York sugar, coffee and cocoa differ in delivery and grade, so they can diverge; the manager trades all of them and splits risk.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C07-3|MT207-C07-3]] — A COMEX copper long exited around 4.6 after a tariff gap (theoretical exit north of 5.17) because there are no resting stops and exits are delayed.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C08-1|MT207-C08-1]] — Signals use the last settlement and trade the next day settlement, with no stop orders, to avoid being picked off.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C08-3|MT207-C08-3]] — Flash spikes (e.g. South African rand) can trigger resting stops; delayed exits avoid being kicked out by one-off glitches.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C09-3|MT207-C09-3]] — About 70% of trades lose and 30% win, so the entry signal quality is weak and profit comes from outliers.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C10-1|MT207-C10-1]] — Profit comes from keeping losses small via initial position size, stop placement and exit on give-back, not from entries.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C10-2|MT207-C10-2]] — Key drivers of trend success, in order: diversification across independent markets, small losses and letting profits run, and sizing.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C10-4|MT207-C10-4]] — Keeping losses small avoids volatility drag that makes drawdowns hard to recover from.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C11-2|MT207-C11-2]] — Vol targeting gives smoother returns and fewer big losses but 'something has to give'.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C11-3|MT207-C11-3]] — Initial trade sizing is the most important risk control; later size changes tend to be profit-taking trades.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C12-1|MT207-C12-1]] — Frequent vol-based resizing turns a trend system into mean reversion, adds commissions and bid-offer costs, and cuts the right-tail outliers (gold, livestock this year) that make the year.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C12-2|MT207-C12-2]] — Risk-based DPS would trim a long gold position as the price moves away from its exit level, giving back asymmetry; the trade-off is smoother returns and lower drawdowns vs fewer outlier years.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C12-3|MT207-C12-3]] — Moritz argues for 'punchy', lopsided returns in stress events, accepting larger give-back potential.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C13-1|MT207-C13-1]] — Blending managers with different position-sizing approaches diversifies the process even when long-term correlation is high.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C13-X1|MT207-C13-X1]] — Niels counters the 'punchiness' case: not resizing leaves event risk (a position can be stopped out on a one-or-two-day move), while vol-based sizing trims positions in vol bursts but rebuilds them when vol falls, so the difference is more nuanced than portrayed.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C14-1|MT207-C14-1]] — In big events correlated behavior means more markets with positions give a larger loss; the firm does not force a position in every market.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C15-X1|MT207-C15-X1]] — Volatility is a poor risk measure for investors; the real risk is the loss one can experience, so the study compares maximum drawdown relative to volatility, where trend following ranks first.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C16-2|MT207-C16-2]] — Volatility is not risk for trend followers; it creates opportunity, and hedge fund realized vol has dropped to about half of previous levels.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C18-2|MT207-C18-2]] — Strategies that look safe standalone are highly correlated in crises with the assets they diversify.

## [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro]]
- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C05-3|MT208-C05-3]] — How high yields can go depends on the dollar: with DXY 96-100 and above the bond market gets close to breaking (stocks, oil and bonds sell together).
- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C08-1|MT208-C08-1]] — Yields are far below the 40-year downtrend line yet markets are already struggling, which shows how little rate increase the system can bear.
- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C20-X1|MT208-C20-X1]] — Aggressive end-game bets such as 50% Bitcoin are viable only for owners without short-term mandates or quarterly reporting; others have to manage position size and drawdown tolerance because losses of 30% would force exits.

## [[MT209 - What is the fiscal rule-]]
- [[MT209 - What is the fiscal rule-#^MT209-C02-2|MT209-C02-2]] — Holding most of the national wealth in equities creates a new risk: market falls feed straight into the budget and economy.

## [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390]]
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C01-2|MT210-C01-2]] — Stagflation fear from the oil shock made equities and bonds fall together, removing the usual bond hedge; 10y yields rebounded from below 4%.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C03-2|MT210-C03-2]] — AlphaQuest closed despite a drawdown within its historical range, showing the business decision to return money rather than a statistical failure.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C04-2|MT210-C04-2]] — Jerry Parker's point: with Bitcoin's huge vol a fixed-size trend system cannot build a meaningful position, and a dynamic position size firm could add as vol falls.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C05-2|MT210-C05-2]] — Source 1 of differentiation: trend dynamically sizes bond/equity exposure (short bonds and less equity in 2022) instead of holding a static 60/40.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C07-1|MT210-C07-1]] — Dispersion between CTAs was driven by position sizing in metals; more static sizing helped on the upside, with opportunities broadening across equities, metals and bonds.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C09-2|MT210-C09-2]] — Dave Dredge's question: investors adapting portfolios should ask what pays off if their view is wrong.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C14-4|MT210-C14-4]] — 2025 reconfirmed trend is diversification, not insurance: it can lose in the short run.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C17-2|MT210-C17-2]] — Allocating to separate specialist managers brings different IP and perspective at the cost of fee netting; solutions businesses growth vs niche houses.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C18-1|MT210-C18-1]] — One or two long-tenured trend followers may beat chasing the smoothest ride.

## [[MT211 - When Prices Stop Making Sense - Systematic Investor - Ep.378]]
- [[MT211 - When Prices Stop Making Sense - Systematic Investor - Ep.378#^MT211-C03-4|MT211-C03-4]] — Risk has a measurable part (volatility) and an unmeasurable part (uncertainty, e.g. Fed dissents or policy changes with tiny samples) that is hard to turn into a model input.
- [[MT211 - When Prices Stop Making Sense - Systematic Investor - Ep.378#^MT211-C04-4|MT211-C04-4]] — A safe asset is defined by diversification; if bonds no longer diversify equities, investors must find other diversifiers such as trend following.
- [[MT211 - When Prices Stop Making Sense - Systematic Investor - Ep.378#^MT211-C14-1|MT211-C14-1]] — Total portfolio approach (e.g. CalPERS) replaces fixed strategic weights with flexible adjustment toward overall return or volatility goals.
- [[MT211 - When Prices Stop Making Sense - Systematic Investor - Ep.378#^MT211-C14-2|MT211-C14-2]] — The 60/40 portfolio may not provide enough diversification, so investors seek alternatives.
- [[MT211 - When Prices Stop Making Sense - Systematic Investor - Ep.378#^MT211-C14-3|MT211-C14-3]] — A 30% overlay of the SG Trend index on a 70/30 stock-bond portfolio yields about 4% tracking error, matching the CalPERS TPA target, a simple way to make a static portfolio adaptive.
- [[MT211 - When Prices Stop Making Sense - Systematic Investor - Ep.378#^MT211-C14-4|MT211-C14-4]] — Trend following already allocates dynamically across and within asset complexes, covering much of what TPA wants.
- [[MT211 - When Prices Stop Making Sense - Systematic Investor - Ep.378#^MT211-C14-5|MT211-C14-5]] — Portfolio weights should respond to changes in volatility and correlation rather than staying fixed.
- [[MT211 - When Prices Stop Making Sense - Systematic Investor - Ep.378#^MT211-C15-1|MT211-C15-1]] — Diversification is the foundation of portfolio construction; start from a strategic allocation and adapt with market conditions.

## [[MT212 - Why Cooperation Is Essential in Crisis - IMF Managing Director Kristalina Georgieva]]
- [[MT212 - Why Cooperation Is Essential in Crisis - IMF Managing Director Kristalina Georgieva#^MT212-C04-3|MT212-C04-3]] — Climate is a financial risk through climate-vulnerable assets and policy changes, and through vulnerable communities (e.g. Philippines, Vietnam).
- [[MT212 - Why Cooperation Is Essential in Crisis - IMF Managing Director Kristalina Georgieva#^MT212-C06-3|MT212-C06-3]] — Climate shocks must be built into budget plans of exposed countries such as Jamaica, as the IMF treats climate as a macro-financial factor when material.

## [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35]]
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C03-2|MT213-C03-2]] — 60/40 fails when bonds stop being defensive (1994, 2022) after sudden bond moves, although 60/40 remains a long-term core.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C03-3|MT213-C03-3]] — Institutions can now obtain diversification through overlays, convexity or downside protection and return stacking; retail has access to derivative income and protection products.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C05-2|MT213-C05-2]] — A tactical inflation-risk overlay shrank active exposures while staying pro-risk.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C05-3|MT213-C05-3]] — Commodities are the best liquid inflation hedge, performing especially in inflation shocks, though they are the 'forgotten stepchild' of multi-asset; equities hedge only up to a threshold.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C06-X1|MT213-C06-X1]] — The multi-asset portfolio is built to balance exposure to growth, inflation and relative valuation views together rather than being a pure growth or pure inflation bet.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C08-3|MT213-C08-3]] — In COVID, data were released late and inputs (e.g. for cruise lines, airlines) did not update, requiring manual intervention; the investment philosophy comes before the model.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C09-1|MT213-C09-1]] — Outright puts are expensive; downside protection is better paid for by replacing equity with long-dated call exposure and rebalancing.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C09-2|MT213-C09-2]] — Defensive equity strategies and sector rotation are other cheaper dynamic downside protection routes.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C09-3|MT213-C09-3]] — Long-volatility protection paid in COVID but not in 2022 because vol rose moderately without spiking while equities fell.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C11-1|MT213-C11-1]] — PGIM keeps a human in the loop, with portfolio managers reviewing trades for consistency with the investment philosophy.

## [[MT214 - Why Macro Investing Matters More Than Ever - Kenneth Tropin on Markets, Volatility & Oppor]]
- [[MT214 - Why Macro Investing Matters More Than Ever - Kenneth Tropin on Markets, Volatility & Oppor#^MT214-C02-3|MT214-C02-3]] — Since the GFC, macro funds typically protected or gained in quarters when equities fell.
- [[MT214 - Why Macro Investing Matters More Than Ever - Kenneth Tropin on Markets, Volatility & Oppor#^MT214-C03-1|MT214-C03-1]] — Macro funds did well in some bad periods (2008, 1987, 2003 tech bust) but cannot promise to, since they can be long equities when they fall.
- [[MT214 - Why Macro Investing Matters More Than Ever - Kenneth Tropin on Markets, Volatility & Oppor#^MT214-C03-2|MT214-C03-2]] — Tactical long/short flexibility plus trading FX and commodities that are uncorrelated to equities makes macro diversifying.
- [[MT214 - Why Macro Investing Matters More Than Ever - Kenneth Tropin on Markets, Volatility & Oppor#^MT214-C03-3|MT214-C03-3]] — Macro aims to be uncorrelated rather than negatively correlated; it was negatively correlated to stocks in about two thirds to three quarters of the bad periods.
- [[MT214 - Why Macro Investing Matters More Than Ever - Kenneth Tropin on Markets, Volatility & Oppor#^MT214-C04-8|MT214-C04-8]] — Graham added discretionary trading in 1998 because quant alone was hit or miss; blending quant and discretionary diversifies.
- [[MT214 - Why Macro Investing Matters More Than Ever - Kenneth Tropin on Markets, Volatility & Oppor#^MT214-C05-3|MT214-C05-3]] — Large multi-strategy funds hold only a small macro sleeve, so they cannot substitute for a dedicated macro allocation.
- [[MT214 - Why Macro Investing Matters More Than Ever - Kenneth Tropin on Markets, Volatility & Oppor#^MT214-C05-X1|MT214-C05-X1]] — Instead of copying multi-strategy platforms, a dedicated macro manager can compete by building custom exposure for each client (discretionary or quant blend, leverage level, volatility target), which gives the allocator a specific macro exposure that a multi-strat sleeve cannot.
- [[MT214 - Why Macro Investing Matters More Than Ever - Kenneth Tropin on Markets, Volatility & Oppor#^MT214-C06-1|MT214-C06-1]] — Graham's discretionary teams averaged about 10% pairwise correlation, higher in turbulent periods; different time horizons keep correlation low.
- [[MT214 - Why Macro Investing Matters More Than Ever - Kenneth Tropin on Markets, Volatility & Oppor#^MT214-C06-2|MT214-C06-2]] — Balance participating in big moves (gold, Bitcoin, weak dollar) with avoiding concentration.
- [[MT214 - Why Macro Investing Matters More Than Ever - Kenneth Tropin on Markets, Volatility & Oppor#^MT214-C06-X1|MT214-C06-X1]] — Multi-strategy pod platforms give each team capital and risk limits and discourage collaboration to keep the correlation between portfolio teams as low as possible, whereas Graham deliberately encourages collaboration while still keeping low team correlation.

## [[MT215 - Why Most Investors Fail at Trend Following - Systematic Investor - Ep.404]]
- [[MT215 - Why Most Investors Fail at Trend Following - Systematic Investor - Ep.404#^MT215-C06-2|MT215-C06-2]] — Combining managed futures with equities is statistically about as obvious as it gets (1+1=3 or 4), with offerings from Fidelity/Crochet, ManHL, Hoffstein, JP Morgan, Winton and Aspect.
- [[MT215 - Why Most Investors Fail at Trend Following - Systematic Investor - Ep.404#^MT215-C07-3|MT215-C07-3]] — Adding a decent managed futures program to any portfolio raises returns, lowers volatility and reduces drawdowns.
- [[MT215 - Why Most Investors Fail at Trend Following - Systematic Investor - Ep.404#^MT215-C07-4|MT215-C07-4]] — Owning a large equity position is comfortable when paired with a long-convexity trend component that fills drawdowns.
- [[MT215 - Why Most Investors Fail at Trend Following - Systematic Investor - Ep.404#^MT215-C08-2|MT215-C08-2]] — Size equity and trend sleeves for equal volatility contribution (e.g. 100% of an 8% vol managed futures program plus 50% of 16% vol stocks) because the better performer is unknown.
- [[MT215 - Why Most Investors Fail at Trend Following - Systematic Investor - Ep.404#^MT215-C09-1|MT215-C09-1]] — Choose the volatility level that is competitive but survivable: double risk invites VaR and compliance stress, half risks lagging inflation and fees.
- [[MT215 - Why Most Investors Fail at Trend Following - Systematic Investor - Ep.404#^MT215-C12-3|MT215-C12-3]] — In April 2020 simple trend rules kept the fund short crude oil when prices went negative, showing blunt tools handle unprecedented events.
- [[MT215 - Why Most Investors Fail at Trend Following - Systematic Investor - Ep.404#^MT215-C13-2|MT215-C13-2]] — Overweighting uncorrelated markets via correlation, cointegration or copula optimization reduced profit and understated risk in live trading.
- [[MT215 - Why Most Investors Fail at Trend Following - Systematic Investor - Ep.404#^MT215-C17-3|MT215-C17-3]] — Combining 0.4-Sharpe strategies (equities, managed futures, tail hedging) gives a combined Sharpe of 1.1-1.2, sustainability uncertain but closer to 1 than any single strategy.
- [[MT215 - Why Most Investors Fail at Trend Following - Systematic Investor - Ep.404#^MT215-C17-4|MT215-C17-4]] — A Man Group paper ('Don't look down') argues what matters for drawdowns is what else is drawing down at the same time.
- [[MT215 - Why Most Investors Fail at Trend Following - Systematic Investor - Ep.404#^MT215-C18-2|MT215-C18-2]] — Managed futures is a dynamic, tactical asset class whereas other portfolio assets are static betas; because of low correlation all returns show up as alpha.
- [[MT215 - Why Most Investors Fail at Trend Following - Systematic Investor - Ep.404#^MT215-C18-4|MT215-C18-4]] — 50-70% of trend trades lose, so keep losses small and hold winners for years.

## [[MT216 - Why Smart Investors Are Switching to Trend Following - Systematic Investor 334]]
- [[MT216 - Why Smart Investors Are Switching to Trend Following - Systematic Investor 334#^MT216-C03-3|MT216-C03-3]] — Systematic strategy design should be more conservative than historical data suggest because externalities such as who else trades the same signal with the same parameters cannot be quantified.
- [[MT216 - Why Smart Investors Are Switching to Trend Following - Systematic Investor 334#^MT216-C08-1|MT216-C08-1]] — Contractual hedges such as long-volatility or option structures cover sharp corrections but not an equity-bond correlation shock or prolonged drawdowns, where trend following helps.
- [[MT216 - Why Smart Investors Are Switching to Trend Following - Systematic Investor 334#^MT216-C08-2|MT216-C08-2]] — A defensive overlay should combine contractual hedges (short delta, long vol), dispersion, trend following and carry or market-neutral components, since each covers a different drawdown type.
- [[MT216 - Why Smart Investors Are Switching to Trend Following - Systematic Investor 334#^MT216-C09-2|MT216-C09-2]] — Put hedging requires implementation choices (frequency, splitting notional over days, strike, financing with another option) and becomes complex once multiple components need sizing against the portfolio.
- [[MT216 - Why Smart Investors Are Switching to Trend Following - Systematic Investor 334#^MT216-C10-2|MT216-C10-2]] — Safe equities (top 30% by safety) fell far less than the market in the 2000-02 bear market (-1.7% vs about -14.7%), but still lost money so they are not a bond replacement.
- [[MT216 - Why Smart Investors Are Switching to Trend Following - Systematic Investor 334#^MT216-C10-3|MT216-C10-3]] — A true defensive allocation should produce absolute positive returns in a down market, which safe equities with beta around 0.8 do not.
- [[MT216 - Why Smart Investors Are Switching to Trend Following - Systematic Investor 334#^MT216-C11-1|MT216-C11-1]] — Portfolios believed to be diversified are 85-90% equity risk in disguise (long-short equity, private equity), so true diversification needs other asset classes.
- [[MT216 - Why Smart Investors Are Switching to Trend Following - Systematic Investor 334#^MT216-C15-2|MT216-C15-2]] — Systematic managers should avoid month-end and quarter-end rebalancing schedules: rebalance frequently, consider where liquidity sits and partially rebalance using thresholds.
- [[MT216 - Why Smart Investors Are Switching to Trend Following - Systematic Investor 334#^MT216-C15-3|MT216-C15-3]] — Trend following acts systematically and off the calendar, so it can run alongside slow, committee-driven asset-allocation portfolios and avoid front-running.

## [[MT217 - Why This Market “Melt-Up” is Making Me Nervous - The Weekly Wrap]]
- [[MT217 - Why This Market “Melt-Up” is Making Me Nervous - The Weekly Wrap#^MT217-C02-X2|MT217-C02-X2]] — Despite the froth and the inflation signs, the speaker does not forecast a recession because there is no evidence of one yet; he trims risk (high flyers) instead of making a market call.

## [[MT218 - Why Trend Following Is Harder Than It Looks - Systematic Investor - Ep.405]]
- [[MT218 - Why Trend Following Is Harder Than It Looks - Systematic Investor - Ep.405#^MT218-C03-3|MT218-C03-3]] — A trend follower running only 9% annualized risk was positioned long equities (pro-deal) and flat energies, i.e. the portfolio mostly reflects the market's recent priced-in optimism.
- [[MT218 - Why Trend Following Is Harder Than It Looks - Systematic Investor - Ep.405#^MT218-C05-4|MT218-C05-4]] — Skew is useful for classifying strategies into trend-like (positive skew) and carry-like (negative skew) buckets, because e.g. emerging-market FX carry has negative skew while developed bond carry is mildly positive.
- [[MT218 - Why Trend Following Is Harder Than It Looks - Systematic Investor - Ep.405#^MT218-C07-2|MT218-C07-2]] — If trend following is a sleeve of a 60/40 portfolio, make it more neutral (long only when trend is strong) to avoid double-counting equity and bond premia.
- [[MT218 - Why Trend Following Is Harder Than It Looks - Systematic Investor - Ep.405#^MT218-C08-1|MT218-C08-1]] — Do not distort a trend program to fit the rest of the portfolio; adjust the other holdings, because distorting sectors changes the interaction among all markets.
- [[MT218 - Why Trend Following Is Harder Than It Looks - Systematic Investor - Ep.405#^MT218-C08-3|MT218-C08-3]] — Within a CTA sleeve, tilt sector allocation away from financials (already held) toward commodities for better portfolio diversification.
- [[MT218 - Why Trend Following Is Harder Than It Looks - Systematic Investor - Ep.405#^MT218-C08-X1|MT218-C08-X1]] — Rebuttal to the host: investors who can only allocate 5-20% to a CTA sleeve (not restructure their 60/40) get a better total portfolio if the sleeve carries less equity and bond beta; the ideal is a bigger CTA bucket, but that is often not an option. Niels counters that with only 5% in trend the sleeve's equity exposure is not worth worrying about.
- [[MT218 - Why Trend Following Is Harder Than It Looks - Systematic Investor - Ep.405#^MT218-C12-3|MT218-C12-3]] — An investor in a fund below its high-water mark gets the first returns without performance fees, unless in a separate share class with its own high-water mark.
- [[MT218 - Why Trend Following Is Harder Than It Looks - Systematic Investor - Ep.405#^MT218-C12-X1|MT218-C12-X1]] — Dropping markets that have not trended recently is dangerous: cocoa was idle for 15 years and then produced its biggest trend ever, so rotating only into the best recent performers would have missed it.
- [[MT218 - Why Trend Following Is Harder Than It Looks - Systematic Investor - Ep.405#^MT218-C15-2|MT218-C15-2]] — A reputable study found commodities are the most consistent trend sector in crises, so a portfolio should include many truly uncorrelated markets rather than overweight liquid financial markets.
- [[MT218 - Why Trend Following Is Harder Than It Looks - Systematic Investor - Ep.405#^MT218-C19-2|MT218-C19-2]] — Man paper: examine joint drawdown behaviour across assets as a more rigorous crisis-alpha test than cherry-picked stress periods (e.g. omitting COVID, when trend followers did less well).

## [[MT219 - Why Trend Following Might Outlast the AI Arms Race - Mark Rzepczynski - Systematic Investo]]
- [[MT219 - Why Trend Following Might Outlast the AI Arms Race - Mark Rzepczynski - Systematic Investo#^MT219-C13-2|MT219-C13-2]] — Low correlation between trend positions allows more risk-taking and strong performance; when highly correlated, reduce risk because reversal risk rises.

## [[MT220 - Why Trend Following Never Looks the Same Twice - Systematic Investor - Ep.365]]
- [[MT220 - Why Trend Following Never Looks the Same Twice - Systematic Investor - Ep.365#^MT220-C08-1|MT220-C08-1]] — Return dispersion between trend managers is greater in high-volatility years (2008, 2022) simply because moves are bigger.
- [[MT220 - Why Trend Following Never Looks the Same Twice - Systematic Investor - Ep.365#^MT220-C08-2|MT220-C08-2]] — Combining two or three managers removes much of the return dispersion versus the index, but residual 3-4% (replication 500-600bp slippage) always remains.
- [[MT220 - Why Trend Following Never Looks the Same Twice - Systematic Investor - Ep.365#^MT220-C11-2|MT220-C11-2]] — Investors should start simple: pick two or three different CTAs, avoid analysis paralysis, because CTAs are among few alternatives without hidden equity beta and no style factor persists.
- [[MT220 - Why Trend Following Never Looks the Same Twice - Systematic Investor - Ep.365#^MT220-C11-X1|MT220-C11-X1]] — Combine a faster manager (for crisis alpha) with a slower manager or a replication product, since each style does better in different environments and no style factor persists.

## [[MT221 - Why Trend Following Still Works in Tough Markets - Systematic Investor - Ep.381]]
- [[MT221 - Why Trend Following Still Works in Tough Markets - Systematic Investor - Ep.381#^MT221-C02-3|MT221-C02-3]] — CTA exposure should be chosen to hedge the allocator's existing asset-class exposure (fixed-income holders want fixed-income CTA, equity holders something different).
- [[MT221 - Why Trend Following Still Works in Tough Markets - Systematic Investor - Ep.381#^MT221-C05-1|MT221-C05-1]] — Long volatility is like brakes on a race car: although it earns about zero in the long run, it lets the portfolio keep risk on in the straights and so raises geometric performance, rather than merely slowing it.
- [[MT221 - Why Trend Following Still Works in Tough Markets - Systematic Investor - Ep.381#^MT221-C12-1|MT221-C12-1]] — Managed futures maximum drawdown is about 15-16% over 25 years, much smaller than equities, so perceived risk exceeds actual risk.
- [[MT221 - Why Trend Following Still Works in Tough Markets - Systematic Investor - Ep.381#^MT221-C12-X1|MT221-C12-X1]] — Bonds went about 20 years without a meaningful drawdown and then suffered a drawdown of 15-16%, similar in size to the maximum drawdown of managed futures over 25 years, which undercuts the view that CTAs are riskier than traditional assets.
- [[MT221 - Why Trend Following Still Works in Tough Markets - Systematic Investor - Ep.381#^MT221-C14-1|MT221-C14-1]] — A Sharpe-1 strategy run at half Kelly implies roughly 40-50% annualised volatility, so high-vol CTA products are feasible and could be sold to retail at higher fees as a proportion of vol (offered half in jest).
- [[MT221 - Why Trend Following Still Works in Tough Markets - Systematic Investor - Ep.381#^MT221-C16-1|MT221-C16-1]] — Wealth portfolios should hold a balanced, diversified portfolio and then apply leverage to reach the desired risk, rather than adding equities for risk.

## [[MT222 - Why Trend Following Works - Systematic Investor - Ep.391]]
- [[MT222 - Why Trend Following Works - Systematic Investor - Ep.391#^MT222-C02-X1|MT222-C02-X1]] — Risk planning has to include events that seem unimaginable; the oil spike from 66 to 119 followed by a 30% reversal is one visualisation of such an event, which standard risk models would treat as impossible.
- [[MT222 - Why Trend Following Works - Systematic Investor - Ep.391#^MT222-C03-1|MT222-C03-1]] — Trend models wait patiently; cocoa took about 15 years to deliver, so diversification of investment process matters for the 99% of portfolio that otherwise relies on opinions.
- [[MT222 - Why Trend Following Works - Systematic Investor - Ep.391#^MT222-C03-2|MT222-C03-2]] — Portfolios should be built for being wrong; trend following provides a non-correlated stream that pays off when forecasts fail.
- [[MT222 - Why Trend Following Works - Systematic Investor - Ep.391#^MT222-C05-X1|MT222-C05-X1]] — In early March 2026 the oil shock hit stocks (MSCI World about -2.8% month to date, flat for the year) and bonds (US aggregate about -1.2%) together, so bonds gave no hedge; the SG CTA and Trend indices fell only about 2.1% on the month and were still up about 6.1% for the year, with energy gains offsetting equity, currency and fixed income losses.
- [[MT222 - Why Trend Following Works - Systematic Investor - Ep.391#^MT222-C09-1|MT222-C09-1]] — Five-sigma daily moves occur 5,791 times more often than a normal distribution predicts, with a power-law tail exponent of about 3.33 in every liquid market.
- [[MT222 - Why Trend Following Works - Systematic Investor - Ep.391#^MT222-C10-1|MT222-C10-1]] — Trend systems with dynamic position sizing reduce exposure as volatility expands, so they may sell during a spike and dampen rather than amplify; this differs from vol-buying approaches.
- [[MT222 - Why Trend Following Works - Systematic Investor - Ep.391#^MT222-C11-1|MT222-C11-1]] — Volatility targeting assumes recent vol predicts the future and is calibrated to the middle of the distribution; with tail exponent 3.33 it underestimates the chance of losses, e.g. oil sized for the calm 66-80 range then hitting 119.
- [[MT222 - Why Trend Following Works - Systematic Investor - Ep.391#^MT222-C11-X1|MT222-C11-X1]] — Dunn targets a daily risk level rather than a fixed volatility target, while many managers still use a fixed volatility target; the host argues this distinction matters because of the fat-tailed return distribution.
- [[MT222 - Why Trend Following Works - Systematic Investor - Ep.391#^MT222-C12-3|MT222-C12-3]] — Risk frameworks based on bell curves (position sizing, VaR, 2-3 sigma stress tests) understate risk; diversification across asset classes is participation in different feedback environments with the same structure.

## [[MT223 - Why Trend Thrives When Inflation Returns - Systematic Investor - Ep.373]]
- [[MT223 - Why Trend Thrives When Inflation Returns - Systematic Investor - Ep.373#^MT223-C12-X2|MT223-C12-X2]] — Trend following bleeds a little money day by day like an option-buying strategy and then pays spectacularly when many markets trend (stairs down, elevator up, as in 2021-22 with 60% winners), so the right response is to trust and improve the process rather than abandon it.
- [[MT223 - Why Trend Thrives When Inflation Returns - Systematic Investor - Ep.373#^MT223-C16-1|MT223-C16-1]] — Spiky returns of a QIS trend index signal weak risk management; QIS makes money from netting flows so their execution incentives differ from a fund, though they offer low fees, customisation and capital-efficient margining.

## [[MT224 - Why the Best Portfolios Are Built to Be Wrong - Systematic Investor - Ep. 411]]
- [[MT224 - Why the Best Portfolios Are Built to Be Wrong - Systematic Investor - Ep. 411#^MT224-C02-1|MT224-C02-1]] — Daily-rebalanced 2x levered ETFs suffer volatility drag: SK Hynix ETF fell 47% over three months while the stock ended +1% after a +125% peak, so over long horizons levered ETFs approach zero.
- [[MT224 - Why the Best Portfolios Are Built to Be Wrong - Systematic Investor - Ep. 411#^MT224-C03-2|MT224-C03-2]] — Because the future path is unknowable (infinite paths around a strange attractor), investors should understand current initial conditions and build resilience (brakes) rather than forecast.
- [[MT224 - Why the Best Portfolios Are Built to Be Wrong - Systematic Investor - Ep. 411#^MT224-C08-3|MT224-C08-3]] — Portfolio risk missed is mostly correlation and volatility assumed constant in MPT/EMH frameworks; implicit leverage via volatility selling creates mispriced tails.
- [[MT224 - Why the Best Portfolios Are Built to Be Wrong - Systematic Investor - Ep. 411#^MT224-C10-1|MT224-C10-1]] — Investing is non-ergodic: the ensemble average does not equal the time average (geometric vs arithmetic mean), sequencing matters, and ruin ends the game (Peters, Taleb).
- [[MT224 - Why the Best Portfolios Are Built to Be Wrong - Systematic Investor - Ep. 411#^MT224-C10-X1|MT224-C10-X1]] — External cash flows on the wealth path (taxes taken out, subscriptions or redemptions) change the path of the accumulated balance in a multiplicative process, so investors' real compounding differs from the additive returns reported by the manager.
- [[MT224 - Why the Best Portfolios Are Built to Be Wrong - Systematic Investor - Ep. 411#^MT224-C11-2|MT224-C11-2]] — Volatility drag widens the gap between geometric and arithmetic paths in a compounding way, so wealth is a multiplicative process and arithmetic returns mislead.
- [[MT224 - Why the Best Portfolios Are Built to Be Wrong - Systematic Investor - Ep. 411#^MT224-C13-2|MT224-C13-2]] — Volatility-targeted sizing mechanically raises positions in calm and behavioural leverage-up adds to it; Japanese government bonds in 2018 had 9% trailing vol authorising 16.4x leverage, so a 6% move cost 9% of capital.
- [[MT224 - Why the Best Portfolios Are Built to Be Wrong - Systematic Investor - Ep. 411#^MT224-C13-3|MT224-C13-3]] — High Sharpe ratios authorise more leverage, which is dangerous because the estimates are noisy and tails fat.
- [[MT224 - Why the Best Portfolios Are Built to Be Wrong - Systematic Investor - Ep. 411#^MT224-C15-1|MT224-C15-1]] — Kelly-optimal sizing sits at a peak where overbetting quickly causes ruin, assumes perfect information, and uses estimates whose required sample explodes with fat tails; under-betting gives room for error.
- [[MT224 - Why the Best Portfolios Are Built to Be Wrong - Systematic Investor - Ep. 411#^MT224-C17-1|MT224-C17-1]] — Parking the bus is like a covered call or 60/40: capping upside does not remove tail exposure; you must be convex on both sides where it matters.
- [[MT224 - Why the Best Portfolios Are Built to Be Wrong - Systematic Investor - Ep. 411#^MT224-C18-1|MT224-C18-1]] — October 2008 was 3.4% of total trend-portfolio wealth over 40 years yet the Sharpe ratio moved only from 914 to 931 (2 cents in the dollar), so Sharpe penalises convex strategies.
- [[MT224 - Why the Best Portfolios Are Built to Be Wrong - Systematic Investor - Ep. 411#^MT224-C19-1|MT224-C19-1]] — Traditional diversification assumes stable correlation and ignores connectivity; diversification across things that can each lose everything is bad, whereas diversification across positions with unbounded upside (St. Petersburg paradox) is good.
- [[MT224 - Why the Best Portfolios Are Built to Be Wrong - Systematic Investor - Ep. 411#^MT224-C19-X1|MT224-C19-X1]] — A book that looks diversified across many markets can share one hidden dependency (borrow short and lend long across a dozen markets is a single interest-rate bet), like a thousand strands tied to one anchor; diversification must be judged by architecture and dependencies, not by correlation statistics.
- [[MT224 - Why the Best Portfolios Are Built to Be Wrong - Systematic Investor - Ep. 411#^MT224-C21-2|MT224-C21-2]] — Convex Strategies frees clients' capital from poor diversifiers into explicit negative-correlation hedges so clients keep positive drift assets and the combination provides long gamma on the combined portfolio.
- [[MT224 - Why the Best Portfolios Are Built to Be Wrong - Systematic Investor - Ep. 411#^MT224-C22-1|MT224-C22-1]] — When positively convex, additional risk reduces total risk; the price of convexity is driven by non-price-sensitive participants; opposing views are welcome as convexity can be bought on either side.
- [[MT224 - Why the Best Portfolios Are Built to Be Wrong - Systematic Investor - Ep. 411#^MT224-C23-1|MT224-C23-1]] — Long volatility and trend are complementary (different distribution parts and attachment points; trend has wider market breadth, options need efficient vol markets); combining them improves convexity.
- [[MT224 - Why the Best Portfolios Are Built to Be Wrong - Systematic Investor - Ep. 411#^MT224-C24-3|MT224-C24-3]] — Cap leverage in absolute terms to break the automatic link between a quiet market and a large position, since most capital destruction comes from too much leverage in quiet markets.
- [[MT224 - Why the Best Portfolios Are Built to Be Wrong - Systematic Investor - Ep. 411#^MT224-C25-1|MT224-C25-1]] — Size every market to equal risk instead of tilting to the apparently strongest edge, because edge differences are smaller than estimation noise and tilting rebuilds a single cable and misses cocoa-like explosions.
- [[MT224 - Why the Best Portfolios Are Built to Be Wrong - Systematic Investor - Ep. 411#^MT224-C25-2|MT224-C25-2]] — Grow return by adding unrelated systems/markets rather than scaling existing bets; and enter small, letting open profit build the position.
- [[MT224 - Why the Best Portfolios Are Built to Be Wrong - Systematic Investor - Ep. 411#^MT224-C25-X1|MT224-C25-X1]] — Always enter positions small and let open profit build the position, so the market rather than leverage does the heavy lifting and individual bets stay small relative to estimation error.
- [[MT224 - Why the Best Portfolios Are Built to Be Wrong - Systematic Investor - Ep. 411#^MT224-C26-1|MT224-C26-1]] — Leverage on concave positions (low correlation in good markets, rising in bad) creates volatility drag and disasters; adding non-recourse leverage in the form of options on convex hedges is risk-reducing, enabling portable alpha / return stacking with trend or long vol.
- [[MT224 - Why the Best Portfolios Are Built to Be Wrong - Systematic Investor - Ep. 411#^MT224-C26-2|MT224-C26-2]] — Brennan views long-vol overlays on traditional portfolios as a band-aid; ideally rebuild the portfolio from first principles.
- [[MT224 - Why the Best Portfolios Are Built to Be Wrong - Systematic Investor - Ep. 411#^MT224-C26-X1|MT224-C26-X1]] — Trend following (and by extension long volatility) acts as the enabler that lets investors keep holding equities through drawdowns, yet most investors have not adopted either strategy.
- [[MT224 - Why the Best Portfolios Are Built to Be Wrong - Systematic Investor - Ep. 411#^MT224-C28-1|MT224-C28-1]] — Dredge's closing view: risk is managed by accountability and tacit knowledge, not by magic models; embedding VaR in BIS capital rules removed accountability and invited the GFC.

## [[MT225 - Why the Market Isn’t Moving, And Why That’s Dangerous - Systematic Investor - Ep.389]]
- [[MT225 - Why the Market Isn’t Moving, And Why That’s Dangerous - Systematic Investor - Ep.389#^MT225-C10-X1|MT225-C10-X1]] — Position structure for a secular asset with a bumpy path: keep a structural long in precious metals (never short them) and trade around it with options volatility, adding convex upside only after the expected near-term beta-1 sell-off.

## [[MT226 - Why the Old Rules of Diversification Are Changing - Allocator - Ep.38]]
- [[MT226 - Why the Old Rules of Diversification Are Changing - Allocator - Ep.38#^MT226-C08-3|MT226-C08-3]] — Bonds' role has shifted from duration diversifier to income source, so portfolios should favour income over duration.
- [[MT226 - Why the Old Rules of Diversification Are Changing - Allocator - Ep.38#^MT226-C08-5|MT226-C08-5]] — Diversification is scarce now, so market-neutral low-net alpha strategies have a bigger role.
- [[MT226 - Why the Old Rules of Diversification Are Changing - Allocator - Ep.38#^MT226-C09-2|MT226-C09-2]] — Market-neutral low-net hedge fund strategies should be treated as diversifiers because their zero-beta return stream is less sensitive to growth swings than equity and income exposures.
- [[MT226 - Why the Old Rules of Diversification Are Changing - Allocator - Ep.38#^MT226-C09-3|MT226-C09-3]] — Market-neutral strategies are not as straightforward a diversifier as the 10-year Treasury was a decade ago, but are the best remaining diversification source.
- [[MT226 - Why the Old Rules of Diversification Are Changing - Allocator - Ep.38#^MT226-C10-2|MT226-C10-2]] — A multi-strategy manager can reallocate dynamically across alpha streams, which an end investor combining separate funds cannot replicate; systematic managers have an edge here.
- [[MT226 - Why the Old Rules of Diversification Are Changing - Allocator - Ep.38#^MT226-C10-3|MT226-C10-3]] — Directional macro and long-short equity satellites around a systematic multistrategy core change the risk/return and correlation shape, e.g. for stress environments.
- [[MT226 - Why the Old Rules of Diversification Are Changing - Allocator - Ep.38#^MT226-C10-X1|MT226-C10-X1]] — Large multi-strategy pod-shop hedge funds sell consistent, low-volatility returns and typically show very high Sharpe ratios; the open question raised is whether they still deliver in severe stress or need to be paired with other strategies.
- [[MT226 - Why the Old Rules of Diversification Are Changing - Allocator - Ep.38#^MT226-C11-2|MT226-C11-2]] — Post-quant-crisis, BlackRock systematic neutralises beta and well-known factor exposures (e.g. momentum) to isolate pure alpha, which makes portfolios more resilient.
- [[MT226 - Why the Old Rules of Diversification Are Changing - Allocator - Ep.38#^MT226-C12-1|MT226-C12-1]] — Beta- and factor-neutralised portfolio construction has been more resilient in moderate stress episodes since the GFC.
- [[MT226 - Why the Old Rules of Diversification Are Changing - Allocator - Ep.38#^MT226-C13-2|MT226-C13-2]] — Portable alpha separates beta (index vehicle) from alpha (market-neutral long-short overlay) rather than embedding both in a long-only mandate.
- [[MT226 - Why the Old Rules of Diversification Are Changing - Allocator - Ep.38#^MT226-C14-1|MT226-C14-1]] — Replace 60/40 with growth equity core, income-focused diversified fixed income across public and private markets, and diversifiers such as market-neutral hedge funds and liquid alts.
- [[MT226 - Why the Old Rules of Diversification Are Changing - Allocator - Ep.38#^MT226-C14-2|MT226-C14-2]] — Total portfolio approach builds portfolios around underlying risk drivers or themes (growth/AI, duration, income) rather than asset-class buckets.

## [[MT227 - Yield Storm Ahead- Preparing for a New Era - Systematic Investor 330]]
- [[MT227 - Yield Storm Ahead- Preparing for a New Era - Systematic Investor 330#^MT227-C02-1|MT227-C02-1]] — Commodity prices are driven by hard-to-forecast natural events (weather, fires), so they can only be traded with a rules-based approach.
- [[MT227 - Yield Storm Ahead- Preparing for a New Era - Systematic Investor 330#^MT227-C09-X1|MT227-C09-X1]] — The size of the rates hedge matters: a secular rise in rates damages the value of most assets, so a token position will not offset portfolio-wide losses and the hedge has to be large relative to the book.
- [[MT227 - Yield Storm Ahead- Preparing for a New Era - Systematic Investor 330#^MT227-C11-1|MT227-C11-1]] — Callable municipal bonds hide duration risk: if yields rise 1-2% the call is no longer exercised and effective duration jumps from about 5 to 15 years.
- [[MT227 - Yield Storm Ahead- Preparing for a New Era - Systematic Investor 330#^MT227-C11-2|MT227-C11-2]] — Private equity, private credit and other private assets carry both duration and liquidity risk when rates rise.
- [[MT227 - Yield Storm Ahead- Preparing for a New Era - Systematic Investor 330#^MT227-C15-3|MT227-C15-3]] — Express it with calls in a barbell alongside inflation-oriented positioning, rather than all-in Chinese stocks.
